    52	## 1. Mission & evidence base
    53	
    54	Coding agents write plausible code; checkers make it correct. Four empirical findings from
    55	the 2025–26 verification literature drive the design:
    56	
    57	1. **The generate→check→repair loop works and is improving fast.** Dafny auto-verification
    58	   went from 68% to 96% in one year ([DafnyBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08467) →
    59	   [vericoding benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22908)); Verus/Rust sits at 44% and
    60	   climbing (same source; [AutoVerus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13082) reports ~90% on
    61	   its own bench vs 45% for direct prompting).
    62	2. **Feedback quality is the dominant variable.** With the model held fixed,
    63	   counterexample-guided repair lifts success from 12% to 97% in controlled ablations
    64	   ([CEGR](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.13817)); structured repair values roughly triple
    65	   agent-loop success on identical models ([Ray & Goyal](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.14167v1)).
    66	   Feedback without a witness performs little better than none. Every falsified
    67	   executable claim Ply reports therefore MUST carry a concrete failing input and, where
    68	   possible, a replayable test. (Architecture violations, timeouts, tool errors, and
    69	   surviving mutants have no input witness; they carry spans and evidence of their own
    70	   kind.)
    71	3. **Local success does not compose.** Models whose single-function verification
    72	   approaches 96% collapse below 4% on multi-function programs requiring cross-boundary
    73	   reasoning ([DafnyCOMP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23061): 3.69% at Pass@8 with >99%
    74	   syntax correctness — the failure is semantic, not syntactic). Verification is
    75	   therefore modular by construction: Ply checks every function against its own
    76	   contract, and checks callers against their callees' *contracts*, never their bodies.
    77	4. **Machine-written specs go vacuous.** A spec can verify while claiming nothing.
    78	   Mutation testing is therefore a first-class check: a contract that fails to kill
    79	   mutants is flagged as weak.
    80	
    81	Ply's job: one schema for claims, one router to engines, one JSON result schema, one
    82	worklist. The engines already exist and others maintain them. **We build glue and UX,
    83	never solvers.** A session that finds itself implementing SMT encoding, model checking, or
    84	proof search has gone off-spec — stop.
    85	
    86	## 2. Fixed design decisions (do not relitigate)
    87	
    88	| # | Decision | Rationale |
    89	|---|---|---|
    90	| D1 | Plain stable Rust is the only executable artifact. Specs never compile to code, except Ply-generated harnesses, proof modules, and tests. | Zero migration cost; everything works with bare cargo. |
    91	| D2 | Contracts are written as **`#[ply::requires(...)]` / `#[ply::ensures(...)]`** attributes on the function. The `ply-attrs` macro re-emits the original function unchanged, adding `#[cfg_attr(kani, kani::requires(...))]` (and the ensures equivalent). Under plain cargo the attributes vanish; under `cargo kani` they instrument **the real function** — never a copy. Proof harnesses are generated into a `cfg(kani)`-gated module inside the target crate so they see private items. Pre-existing `#[cfg_attr(kani, kani::requires(...))]` attributes are harvested and merged by conjunction. | Kani's `proof_for_contract` verifies the function the attributes annotate; contracts on a generated copy would verify a different symbol. `cfg_attr` keeps bare `cargo build` working (D1). The in-crate module mechanism (generated file + one module declaration, or an equivalent include) is settled by the M0 spike; the fallback is verifying `pub` items only from a sibling harness crate. |
    92	| D3 | Architecture claims, checks, capabilities, ownership, profiles, and the unresolved registry live in **`ply.yaml`**, validated against a normative JSON Schema (§5). | These claims are cross-cutting and have no natural attribute location. YAML plus a schema needs no parser of our own, and agents emit YAML reliably. The schema, not prose, is the formal definition. |
    93	| D4 | Architecture enforcement has two tiers. **Crate-level dependency rules** (from `cargo metadata`, which is exact) are errors and default-deny between declared components. **Item-level rules** (calls, capabilities, ownership — from syn, which is approximate) are warnings by default; a component opts into item-level errors with `strict: true`. | Default-deny is only honest over facts that are sound. Crate dependency data is sound today; syn-derived call data is not (no name resolution, no macro expansion). Advisory-until-strict gives teeth without theater. |
    94	| D5 | Verification is modular and evidence-honest. Kani's `stub_verified(g)` is used only when `g` itself passed a Kani contract proof this run, in the same crate. Any weaker case — callee merely fuzzed or tested, callee in another crate, cycle in the call graph — verifies the caller against an *assumed* contract and marks the verdict **`conditional`**, listing the assumptions. A conditional verdict never reads as plain `bounded`. | Stubbing is a soundness claim; fuzzing does not license it. Kani contracts are crate-local (the cross-crate wrapper/double-stub workaround is out of scope for v1). Never inline a contracted callee's body. |
    95	| D6 | Verdicts aggregate upward as **worst-of** over the evidence order `violation < unclaimed < tested < fuzzed < bounded < proved`. Statuses (`conditional`, `stale`, `weak-spec`, `unsupported`, `engine-missing`, `timeout`, `inconclusive`) do not sit in that order; they propagate upward as flags and open-item counts alongside it. | A proof in one corner must not hide a merely-tested boundary in another; and a timeout is not a weaker proof, it is a different kind of fact. |
    96	| D7 | Every counterexample is stored as **Kani playback data** (exact, engine-version-bound) and, whenever the inputs can be rendered as stable Rust source, additionally as an ordinary `#[test]` that fails under plain `cargo test`. When rendering fails, the diagnostic says so (`W0541`) — inputs are never fabricated. | Playback is what Kani actually provides; the portable test is the agent-friendly repair target and is emitted whenever honestly possible. |
    97	| D8 | `synth` mode (the model writes the function body) is orchestration over the check pipeline: prompt assembly, a retry loop, and marking the output as derived. It ships last and adds no checking machinery. | Thin by design. |
    98	| D9 | Implementation language Rust; three crates (§4); engines run as subprocesses with version detection at startup, never linked as libraries. The Kani version is pinned in `ply.toml` and recorded in every fingerprint. | Engine version churn must not break our build or silently invalidate old evidence. |
    99	| D10 | `node_id` is the component path + item path only. The blake3 `content_hash` is a sibling field, never part of the ID. All JSON output shares one envelope (§8) containing a tree of nodes plus a flat diagnostics list. | IDs must survive edits so external consumers (a future canvas UI) can track nodes across runs. Design for the canvas now; build it never (out of scope). |
   100	| D11 | Extraction may be incomplete but never silently so: every call site the extractor cannot resolve is counted and reported (W0412 plus a per-component coverage metric in `check` output). | Visibility is what makes the advisory tier (D4) and any future `strict` opt-in meaningful. |
   101	| D12 | A function declares a **checks list**, e.g. `checks: [bounded(3), fuzz(256), mutate]`. `mutate` requires a `test` or `fuzz` entry in the same list (else `E0504`) and uses only those as its mutant-kill signal, scoped per function with cargo-mutants' `--re`. | One base check could not express "bounded plus a fuzz-backed mutation tier". Running Kani once per mutant costs minutes per mutant per function; proof-backed mutation needs an opt-in budget, which is out of scope. |
   102	| D13 | **Spike before build** (milestone M0): every engine-facing detail in this spec is provisional until a hands-on spike, with the pinned Kani version, records in ADR-0003 what actually works — attribute emission, in-crate harness modules, `stub_verified`, playback, input construction. The spec is then amended to match reality. | The engine surface is the highest-risk part of the design; paper decisions there are guesses. |
   103	| D14 | `ply.lock` records, per claim, a **fingerprint**: item token-stream hash, merged contract text, callee contract hashes, engine name + version + flags, active features, target triple. A verdict is `stale` when any part changed. `cargo ply accept` re-blesses fingerprints and refuses (`E0303`) nodes whose last run failed. The fingerprint is also the verification cache key: `verify` skips any node whose fingerprint matches a recorded passing verdict and reuses it; `--force` reruns. | An old success must not bless changed assumptions or a different toolchain; without an accept verb, staleness warnings accumulate until they mean nothing; and without fingerprint-keyed skipping, every CI run re-pays full engine cost and the tool becomes nightly-only. |
   104	
   189	## 5. The `ply.yaml` format
   190	
   191	Files named `ply.yaml` (or `*.ply.yaml`) are discovered from the workspace root downward
   192	(lexicographic path order; `target/` and gitignored paths excluded) and merge into one
   193	model. Merge order cannot matter: duplicate component names are errors. **The JSON Schema
   194	at `schema/ply.schema.json` is the normative definition of the format**; this section is
   195	its prose rendering, and any divergence is a bug in this section. The schema is embedded
   196	in the binary, shipped in the repo, versioned by the top-level `ply: 1` field, and
   197	golden-tested. `cargo ply skill` embeds it in the generated skill file. The embedded
   198	copy is authoritative: Ply never reads a schema from the target workspace, so editing an
   199	on-disk copy changes nothing at runtime — the shipped file exists as read-only reference
   200	and for IDE tooling. The grammar evolves only through Ply releases and the `ply:`
   201	version field.
   202	
   203	Everything is declarative data. The only embedded syntaxes are:
   204	1. **check strings** — `test | fuzz(N)? | bounded(K)? | prove | mutate`.
   205	   Schema-validated by regex, parsed in ply-core.
   206	2. **edge strings** — `A -> B` declares that component A may call component B (checked).
   207	   `A ~> B : path::Type` declares a data flow (parsed and rendered, NOT checked in v1).
   208	3. **deny strings** — `PAT -> PAT [except C1, C2]` where `PAT := IDENT | *`.
   209	4. **Rust expressions** — contract strings restricted to the closed subset (§5.4a);
   210	   example strings are arbitrary Rust expressions (they compile as plain tests).
   211	
   212	### 5.1 Document structure
   213	
   214	```yaml
   215	ply: 1                           # schema version, required
   216	
   217	components:
   218	  pricing:                       # component name (unique across ALL merged files → E0202)
   219	    anchor: pricing              # crate name, or crate::module::path — required
   220	    pure: true                   # optional, default false: no capabilities at all
   221	    strict: false                # optional: item-level violations become errors (D4)
   222	    uses: [time]                 # optional; caps: net fs db time rand proc unsafe
   223	    owns: [pricing::Book]        # optional; only this component may mutate these types
   224	    profile: hot_path            # optional; must name a declared profile
   225	    checks: [bounded(2)]         # optional default checks for all fns in scope
   226	    components:                  # optional nested components, same shape
   227	      curves:
   228	        anchor: pricing::curves
   229	    fns:                         # optional fn claims, keyed by path relative to anchor
   230	      quote:                     # impl methods use Type::method
   231	        checks: [bounded(3), fuzz(256), mutate]
   232	        mode: check              # check (default) | synth
   233	        requires:                # optional; Rust expression strings, ANDed with
   234	          - "inst.tick > 0"      #   any inline attributes
   235	        ensures:
   236	          - "|result| result.bid <= result.ask"
   237	        examples:                # optional; each a Rust `==` expression string
   238	          - "quote(Instrument { id: 1, tick: 2 }).bid == 4"
   239	        check_with: { T: u64 }   # optional; concrete types for generic params (§5.4b)
   240	        trusted:                 # optional; externally-attested claims (§5.4d)
   241	          - { claim: "cross-thread safety", evidence: "loom test tests/loom_quote.rs" }
   242	        unresolved:              # optional registry links for markers in this fn
   243	          - { id: 147, note: "employee discount undecided" }
   244	
   245	edges:
   246	  - pricing -> parser
   247	  - "pricing ~> risk : pricing::Quote"
   248	
   249	deny:
   250	  - "* -> db_raw except migrations"
   251	
   252	profiles:
   253	  hot_path: [no_panics, no_trait_objects]
   254	  # available bans: no_unsafe no_trait_objects no_interior_mut
   255	  #                 no_panics no_async exhaustive_match
   256	
   257	unresolved:                      # registry entries with no code anchor
   258	  - { id: 151, note: "settlement rounding rule TBD" }
   259	```
   260	
   261	Schema violations produce `E0201` diagnostics carrying the JSON-pointer path and source
   262	line. Line mapping: serde derive builds the model; a second lightweight pass over a
   263	position-marked YAML parse builds a JSON-pointer → (line, col) index used only for
   264	diagnostics. Duplicate component names across merged files → `E0202` (nested names are
   265	qualified by their parent, so only siblings can collide). A string that passes the schema
   266	regex but fails the real micro-syntax parser → `E0203`, stating the expected form.
   267	`mutate` without a `test` or `fuzz` entry in the same checks list → `E0504`.
   268	
   269	### 5.1a Strictness & lexical rules
   270	
   271	The schema must encode all of the following; the goldens (§9) pin them.
   272	
   273	1. **Unknown fields are errors.** Every object in the schema sets
   274	   `additionalProperties: false`; an unrecognized key → `E0204` with a
   275	   nearest-known-key suggestion. A typo must be caught, never ignored.
   276	2. **Identifiers.** Component and profile names match `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*` (snake_case,
   277	   ASCII). In edge and deny strings, tokens are separated by one or more spaces; the
   278	   parser accepts any run of whitespace and the canonical form uses single spaces.
   279	3. **Code paths.** Anchors and fn keys are plain segment paths: `IDENT(::IDENT)*`, where
   280	   a segment may also be a type name in `Type::method` position. No generics, no
   281	   trait-qualified paths (`<T as Trait>::f`), no lifetimes. An anchor or fn key outside
   282	   this form → `E0304 unsupported path form`, naming the construct.
   283	4. **Numeric bounds.** `fuzz(N)`: 1 ≤ N ≤ 1_000_000. `bounded(K)`: 1 ≤ K ≤ 64. Out of
   284	   range → `E0203`.
   285	5. **Unresolved ids** are positive integers, unique across the whole merged workspace
   286	   (registry and fn entries together); a duplicate → `E0205`.
   287	6. **Component references** in edge and deny strings use the component namespace, not
   288	   Rust paths: a bare name resolves only if it is unique across the whole merged tree;
   289	   otherwise the dotted qualified form `parent.child` is required. An ambiguous bare
   290	   reference → `E0206`, listing the candidates. (Discovered in vetting: two parents may
   291	   legally have same-named children, so `A -> B` alone can be ambiguous.)
   292	
   293	### 5.2 Anchoring & staleness
   294	
   295	Every component anchors to a real crate or module; every fn claim anchors to a real
   296	function. `ply check` resolves anchors via the extractor. An unresolvable anchor →
   297	`E0301` with nearest-name suggestions (edit distance over the item index). **A renamed
   298	function must break CI, not silently orphan its claims.**
   299	
   300	Each claim's fingerprint (D14) lives in `ply.lock` (committed). When any part of
   301	the fingerprint no longer matches, `ply check` reports `W0302 claim may be stale` and the
   302	node carries status `stale`. `cargo ply accept [node_id ...|--all]` re-records
   303	fingerprints once a human (or an agent, after verifying) confirms the claims still hold;
   304	it refuses (`E0303`) nodes whose last verify run failed.
   305	
   306	### 5.3 Architecture semantics (always-on, M2)
   306	### 5.3 Architecture semantics (always-on, M2)
   307	
   308	**Crate tier (sound, errors).** From `cargo metadata`: the exact crate dependency graph.
   309	A dependency between two crates belonging to different declared components with no `->`
   310	edge → `A0401`. `deny` patterns over components resolve against this graph → `A0405`.
   311	
   312	**Item tier (approximate, warnings; errors under `strict: true`).** From the syn-backed
   313	extractor, behind `trait Extractor` (ADR-0001):
   314	
   315	- `calls(from_item, to_item)` — direct calls, method calls, and fully-qualified calls,
   316	  resolved best-effort through `use` maps and local type declarations.
   317	- `calls_dyn(from, trait)` — dynamic dispatch; reported `W0411` if the trait has
   318	  implementations in a component the caller may not reach.
   319	- `calls_unresolved(from, span)` — call sites the extractor cannot place (D11).
   320	- `touches_cap(item, cap)` — capability approximation: uses of
   321	  `std::net`/`std::fs`/`std::process`/`std::time`, rand crates, and `unsafe` blocks,
   322	  plus a user-extensible `capmap.toml` mapping crate paths to caps.
   323	- `mutates(item, TYPEPATH)` — mutation of a named type.
   324	
   325	Containment implies permission: a component may always call into its own descendants
   326	(and they into it), the same way it calls between its own functions — no edge is
   327	declared for calls within one nesting line. An explicit edge whose endpoints are a
   328	component and its own descendant is redundant → `W0409` (document-local; `ply check`
   329	reports it, and the renderer draws nothing for it — vetting 003's parent-to-child edge
   330	drew as a diagonal slash through the parent's own content, which is what a drawing of
   331	"I may call myself" deserves). Edges are for crossings between nesting lines, including
   332	into another component's descendant (`strategy -> ingest.book`).
   333	
   334	Item-tier rules (each `W`-severity by default, `A`-severity error under `strict`):
   335	1. A call crosses two declared components with no `->` edge → `A0402`.
   336	2. A `pure` component touches any capability → `A0403` (names the cap, spans the item).
   337	3. A component reaches a capability outside its `uses` set through its own code, rather
   338	   than through a declared `->` edge into a component that has the cap → `A0404`.
   339	4. `owns T`: an item outside the owning component mutates `T` → `A0406`.
   340	5. Profile bans (syntactic checks over the component's items — these are reliable and
   341	   always errors) → `A0407`.
   342	
   343	The per-item escape `#[ply::allow(name, reason = "...")]` accepts a ban name or an
   344	item-tier diagnostic code (`A0402`–`A0404`, `A0406`, `A0408`) and suppresses that finding
   345	on that item. Without it, the first false positive from the approximate extractor under
   346	`strict: true` would force the whole component back to advisory. Every escape is recorded
   347	in the audit list.
   348	
   349	**Resolution visibility (D11)**: `check` output reports, per component, the share of call
   350	sites the extractor resolved. An unresolved call site whose textual candidates include an
   351	item in a component that would need an undeclared edge → `W0412 possible undeclared edge
   352	(call unresolved)`. Unresolved sites with no such candidate are counted but not itemized.
   353	SCHEMA.md must state plainly that item-tier facts are approximate and how `strict`
   354	changes severity.
   355	
   356	Edges constrain **direct** calls only: `A -> B` and `B -> C` neither grants nor requires
   357	`A -> C`.
   358	
   359	### 5.4 Contract semantics
   360	
   361	The canonical contract source is the inline `#[ply::requires]`/`#[ply::ensures]`
   362	attributes on the function (D2). `requires`/`ensures` entries in `ply.yaml` are ANDed in,
   363	for teams that prefer external specs. Because the attributes annotate the real function,
   364	rustc type-checks contract expressions whenever the crate builds under `cfg(kani)`; under
   365	plain cargo they are inert.
   366	
   367	#### 5.4a Spec expression subset (contracts only)
   368	
   369	This subset applies to `requires`/`ensures` — the expressions sent to proof engines.
   370	`examples` entries are exempt: they are arbitrary Rust `==` expressions, compiled as
   371	plain `#[test]`s and never translated for an engine.
   372	
   373	Boolean Rust expressions over the function's parameters and `result`; literals (integer,
   374	bool, char, string); calls to `pure`-marked helper fns; `==,!=,<,<=,>,>=`; `&&,||,!`;
   375	arithmetic; field access; `.len()`; `.is_ok()/.is_err()/.is_some()`; `matches!()`. The
   376	list is closed: any construct outside it — indexing, other method calls, paths to
   377	constants, closures other than the `|result| expr` ensures form — is rejected with
   378	`E0501` naming the construct. No side effects (checked syntactically). Identifiers must
   379	resolve to parameters or `result`, sanity-checked against the anchored signature.
   380	
   381	`old(expr)` — the value `expr` had on entry — is admitted in `ensures` only, with `expr`
   382	itself drawn from this subset and `old()` non-nested. It exists because single-state
   383	postconditions cannot correctly specify anything that mutates (vetting 001: the
   384	push/pop contracts flagged correct code without it). Engines: Kani maps it to
   385	`kani::old(expr)`; generated test/fuzz harnesses evaluate `expr` before the call and
   386	substitute the snapshot. `old()` in `requires` is meaningless and rejected (`E0501`).
   387	Full two-state/model-based specs (sequence histories, FIFO ordering) remain out of
   388	scope — `old()` is the single two-state primitive.
   389	
   390	A `#[ply::pure]` helper called from any contract is a trust surface, not a free pass. It
   391	is always checked for capability use — a pure fn that touches any capability is an error
   392	(`A0408`), regardless of `strict` — and every contract-used helper appears in
   393	`cargo ply audit` as a trusted assumption. If the helper does not itself carry a passing
   394	check, every verdict whose contract calls it is `conditional`, listing the helper: an
   395	unverified helper that panics or lies poisons every contract built on it.
   396	
   397	#### 5.4b Supported signatures
   398	
   399	An engine can only check a function whose inputs it can construct. v1 supports functions
   400	whose parameters and return type are, recursively: integers, `bool`, `char`, structs and
   401	enums of supported types with Ply-derivable `Arbitrary` (public, invariant-free fields),
   402	`Option`/`Result`/`Vec` of supported types, and `&T`/`&[T]` of the above (built from an
   403	owned value in the harness). Everything else — trait objects, generics, smart pointers,
   404	non-exhaustive or private-field types without a user-supplied generator — yields status
   405	`unsupported` with diagnostic `V0505` naming the offending type. Unsupported is a
   406	reported fact, never a harness build failure. A user-supplied generator hook (a
   407	`pure`-marked constructor function named in `ply.yaml`) lifts a type into the supported
   408	set; its design is validated in the M0 spike.
   409	
   410	Generic functions are checkable only through a concrete instantiation: `check_with:
   411	{ T: u64 }` names one concrete type per type parameter, and every harness for that fn
   412	instantiates with it. A generic fn without `check_with` is `unsupported` (V0505). One
   413	instantiation per fn in v1; the verdict names it (e.g. `bounded(3) as T=u64`) so nobody
   414	mistakes evidence about one instantiation for evidence about all.
   415	
   416	#### 5.4c Check → engine mapping
   417	
   418	| check | engine | verdict on success |
   419	|---|---|---|
   420	| test | generated `#[test]`s from `examples`, plus generated direct contract cases (concrete inputs run through the real function, contract asserted) | `tested` |
   421	| fuzz(n) | proptest harness, n cases (default 256), shrinking on; `requires` as a rejection filter, with a warning when the rejection rate is high | `fuzzed(n)` |
   422	| bounded(k) | Kani contract proof (`proof_for_contract`), loop bound k (default 2) | `bounded(k)` |
   423	| prove | Verus translation (M7, optional) | `proved` |
   424	| mutate | cargo-mutants scoped `--re <fn>`; kill signal = the `test`/`fuzz` checks in the same list (D12) | appends `·spec-strong`, or flags `W0502 weak spec (N surviving mutants)` |
   425	
   426	cargo-mutants runs the workspace test suite by default, which would never execute the
   427	generated fuzz harnesses under `target/ply/fuzz/` — so the adapter passes a custom test
   428	command that runs exactly the target fn's generated `test`/`fuzz` harnesses. (Mechanism
   429	confirmed in the M0 spike.) Without this, `·spec-strong` would measure the wrong thing.
   430	
   431	There is no transparent runtime enforcement: generated tests call functions explicitly.
   432	An `induct` check (Kani loop contracts, proving loops by invariant instead of unrolling)
   433	is planned, not in v1: Kani's loop-contract support is experimental, and Ply has no
   434	stable-Rust invariant attribute yet. A function's verdict is the strongest evidence its
   435	passing checks earned; a failing check is a `violation` regardless of what else passed.
   436	Default checks: `[bounded(2)]` if the fn has any contract, else none.
   437	
   438	#### 5.4d Trusted claims
   439	
   440	Some load-bearing properties live outside Ply's reach — cross-thread safety proven by a
   441	loom test, a paper proof, an external audit. A `trusted` entry records such a claim with
   442	its evidence: `{ claim, evidence }`. Trusted claims change no verdict and run no engine;
   443	they exist so the tree is honest — without them, a node whose real correctness argument
   444	is external renders indistinguishably green. They appear in `cargo ply audit` as part of
   445	the trust surface and carry a distinct visual form (§7.1). An agent must never add or
   446	edit a `trusted` entry on its own judgment; attestation is a human act.
   447	
   448	### 5.5 Modular composition (D5)
   449	
   450	Verification runs callees-before-callers over the call graph. To verify fn `f` that calls
   451	contracted fn `g`:
   452	
   453	- `g` passed its own Kani contract proof this run, and `g` is in the same crate →
   454	  generate `f`'s harness with `#[kani::stub_verified(g)]`. Clean verdict.
   455	- Anything else — `g` merely fuzzed or tested, `g` in another crate, `f` and `g` in a
   456	  cycle → verify `f` assuming `g`'s contract, and mark `f`'s verdict `conditional`
   457	  (`W0511`), listing each assumed contract.
   458	
   459	The verdict tree shows each verdict's assumption chain; `conditional` propagates upward as a
   460	status (D6). Cross-crate `stub_verified` (Kani's wrapper/double-stub workaround) is out
   461	of scope for v1.
   462	
   463	### 5.6 Underspecification
   464	
   465	`ply::unresolved!(147, "employee discount undecided")` marks a decision nobody has made
   466	yet. It expands to `unimplemented!("unresolved #147: employee discount undecided")` —
   467	always, dev and prod alike. Simple, honest, greppable. `ply worklist` lists every marker
   468	(macro or `ply.yaml` registry) with its span, enclosing component, and blocking status.
   469	A fn containing `unresolved!` is capped at check `test`, flagged `W0521`.
   470	
   471	### 5.7 Synth mode (M6, experimental)
   472	
   473	`mode: synth` on a fn claim means the model writes the body. `cargo ply synth <fn>`:
   474	1. Assemble the generation contract: signature, contracts, examples, the enclosing
   475	   component's caps/edges/profile (as prose constraints plus the raw `ply.yaml` excerpt),
   476	   and the signatures + contracts of everything the fn may call.
   477	2. Invoke the model (pluggable; default is a `claude` CLI subprocess; configured in
   478	   `ply.toml`).
   479	3. Write the body; run the check pipeline for this fn only; on failure, feed the
   480	   Diagnostic JSON back; loop at most N times (default 5).
   481	4. On success, mark the fn `#[ply::derived(spec_hash = "...")]` and record it in the
   482	   verdict tree. When a derived body is later hand-edited (its hash no longer matches), `ply check` warns
   483	   `W0531` and the fn silently becomes `mode: check`.
   484	
   485	No streaming, no IDE integration, no multi-fn synthesis in v1.
   486	
   487	---
   515	## 7. Recursion & aggregation (the zoom model)
   516	
   517	The model is a tree: workspace → components → nested components → fns. Every node
   518	carries `{ id, kind, anchor, content_hash, verdict, statuses, worst_descendant,
   519	open_items }`. `verdict` is the node's own claim status; `worst_descendant` implements
   520	D6 over the evidence order; `statuses` and `open_items` (unresolved markers, weak specs,
   521	conditional or stale verdicts) propagate upward as counts.
   522	
   523	Aggregation rules the verdict kernel (`tools/kernel`) checks exhaustively and the tool
   524	must preserve:
   525	
   526	- **Kinds only.** The evidence order compares the six kinds; the `n`/`k` parameters of
   527	  `fuzzed(n)`/`bounded(k)` are reported in the verdict, never compared. Two claims of
   528	  the same kind are the same rung — the ladder's pressure is toward a stronger kind,
   529	  not a bigger number.
   530	- **Only claimable items contribute their own evidence.** fns fold their own verdict
   531	  into `worst_descendant`; containers (workspace, components) fold over children only.
   532	  A container with no claimable descendants reads `unclaimed`. (The literal uniform
   533	  fold would make every container read `unclaimed` forever, since containers carry no
   534	  claims of their own.)
   535	- **`open_items` counts flag instances, not flagged nodes**: a node carrying two
   536	  statuses contributes 2. The count is triage pressure and must not lose information.
   537	- **`conditional` structurally carries its assumptions** (D5): a conditional status
   538	  without an assumptions list is unrepresentable in the kernel, not validated against.
   539	
   540	`tree --json` emits this tree (D10). Human-facing `tree` renders it as an indented
   541	tree, one line per node, worst first; `--depth N` limits depth and `--focus <id>`
   542	descends into a subtree. This is the human review surface: at depth 1 you see which
   543	subsystem is weakest, then zoom. A future canvas UI renders the same JSON; nothing in
   544	this repo should special-case it beyond keeping the schema stable and treelike.
   545	
   546	### 7.1 The visual grammar
   547	
   548	The grammar is designed to be drawn. Every construct in `ply.yaml` has exactly one
   549	visual form, and the mapping is total in both directions: a diagram of the model shows
   550	nothing that was not declared, and everything declared can be shown. This bijection is a
   551	design gate: **a proposed grammar feature with no clear visual form does not enter the
   552	grammar.**
   553	
   554	| Construct | Visual form |
   555	|---|---|
   556	| component (nesting) | box; nested boxes for nested components; anchor as subtitle |
   557	| fn claim | leaf chip inside its component's box |
   558	| `->` edge | solid arrow between boxes |
   559	| `~>` data flow | dashed arrow labeled with the type |
   560	| deny | barred red arrow between the matched patterns; a `*` pattern draws its own per-rule "any" marker — wildcards have no shared identity, so unrelated rules never appear connected |
   561	| `owns` | header line under the anchor, `owns T, U` — the types this component is sole mutator of |
   562	| capabilities / `pure` | badge row on the box; `pure` = a sealed border, no badges |
   563	| profile | tag on the box |
   564	| checks list | glyph row on the fn chip |
   565	| verdict | node fill on the ordinal scale: `violation` red → `proved` deepest green; `unclaimed` unfilled |
   566	| statuses | corner markers on the node (conditional, stale, weak-spec, …) |
   567	| worst_descendant | a collapsed box takes its weakest descendant's fill — D6 made visible |
   568	| assumption chain | thin dotted arrows from a verdict to the contracts it assumed |
   569	| unresolved marker | numbered pin on the fn or component |
   570	| trusted claim | hollow shield badge on the node — attested by named evidence, not machine-checked |
   571	| contract clauses (`requires`/`ensures`) | a contract mark on the fn chip — a solid ink bar the full height of the chip's left edge (a gutter mark: "this row carries something binding"); the tooltip lists each clause verbatim. This draws the §7.2 watermark per function: marked = a promise stands at the mark; bare = signature only. (The renderer sees only YAML-declared clauses; inline attributes join when `cargo ply` emits the §8 envelope.) |
   572	| declared ceiling | component fill, low-saturation, on the verdict ordinal scale: the strongest verdict the component's declared checks *could* earn — per fn the strongest check kind (`test`→tested … `prove`→proved; `mutate` strengthens, never lifts; no checks = unclaimed, unfilled), folded worst-of by the kernel's container rule. A ceiling is a promise, not proof: it never uses the full-saturation fill reserved for earned verdicts, and its tooltip says none of it has run |
   573	| `strict` | a solid ink triangle notch in the box's top-right corner — the "flagged, zero tolerance" instinct; tooltip already explains errors-not-warnings |
   574	| `mode: synth` | the fn chip's fill turns light violet — violet is hereby the authorship channel, its single meaning "machine-written": the body below the watermark is synthesized from the contract, with the checks holding the line. Tooltip says exactly that |
   575	| `examples` | a gray `e×N` token in the chip's annotation area (next to the `T=...` note); the tooltip already counts and the `test` check runs them |
   576	| hollow component (derived, like findings) | a component that declares nothing inside — no fns, no nested components — draws with a dashed border: a sketch outline, nothing to zoom into yet. Tooltip says so plainly. Derived from absence rather than declared; the natural state of every box in top-down authoring, expected to solidify as claims arrive |
   577	| finding (tool-computed, not declared) | the offending item drawn in error red with an `E####` badge; its tooltip leads with the diagnostic. A finding with no drawable item attaches a red count to the workspace title. A document with findings still renders — a picture that refuses to draw hides the problem it should be showing (origin: fault-injection demo, where a faulted toolchain drew `bounded(0)` as legitimate evidence) |
   578	
   579	A collapsed component is one solid-bordered box (never dashed — hollow means *nothing*
   580	inside; collapsed means *plenty* inside, folded) showing its name, anchor, a contents
   581	line (`N components · M fns`), and its worst-descendant ceiling/verdict fill — and it
   582	draws as a **stack**: one offset card edge behind the box, the "pile of cards" instinct
   583	for folded content. The three depth-states are preattentive: flat card = fully shown,
   584	stacked card = content folded behind, dashed = nothing inside at all. Three
   585	things never fold away: capability badges (union of the subtree's — a collapsed box
   586	containing `net` still shows `net`), the unresolved-pin count, and the finding count.
   587	`ply-render --depth N` and `--focus <component>` mirror the tree CLI.
   588	
   589	Zooming is collapse/expand over the §7 tree and mirrors `tree --depth`/`--focus`. A
   590	renderer's only input is the §8 envelope — no side channel. The renderer itself stays
   591	out of scope; this section fixes what any renderer must show. The archi-techture bundle
   592	in `.archi/` is the working example of the style. One deliberate exception: a minimal
   593	static renderer (`tools/render/`, `ply.yaml` → SVG, no GUI) exists to prove this mapping
   594	is total — every construct drawable, nothing undrawable admitted. It is a spec-validation
   595	tool, not the canvas.
   596	
   597	**Channel discipline (the instinct rule).** The diagram must be readable at a glance by
   598	someone who has learned nothing: every visual channel carries exactly one meaning, and
   599	each meaning borrows an instinct the viewer already has. Hue: red = forbidden or wrong;
   600	green = evidence; amber = a human's attention (owed, or vouched); ink = structure.
   601	Saturation: pastel = promised, saturated = earned. Border: dashed = hollow sketch,
   602	solid = specified, double = sealed pure. Edge dash: solid = may call, dashed = data
   603	flows, red-barred = must not. Small marks: solid square = a contract stands; numbered
   604	amber pin = decision owed; hollow shield = human-attested. A new visual form must draw
   605	from these channels consistently or claim an unused channel and name its single meaning
   606	here — reusing a channel for a second meaning is refused the way an undrawable
   607	construct is. The acceptance check is the **squint test**: blurred, the picture must
   608	still say where the system is weak (pale), wrong (red), unfinished (dashed), and
   609	waiting on a human (amber). A form whose meaning dies under squint is decoration, not
   610	grammar. Words live in tooltips and the frame tooltip; the canvas carries no legend by default —
   611	the picture must work on instinct alone. `ply-render --legend` opts into a compact
   612	legend strip appended below the frame (for docs, onboarding, print), generated from the
   613	same style constants the renderer draws with, so legend and drawing cannot drift apart.
   614	
   615	Gate debt: **none as of 2026-08-23** — every construct in the grammar has an assigned
   616	visual form (the last three, `strict`/`mode: synth`/`examples`, got theirs above).
   617	Renderer implementation status is tracked in TODO.md; an assigned-but-unimplemented
   618	form is scheduled work, not gate debt.
   619	
   620	### 7.2 The watermark
   621	
   622	The system has three strata:
   623	
   624	1. **Declarative** — everything `ply.yaml` and the attributes express: components,
   625	   edges, capabilities, contracts, checks. Fully drawable (§7.1), fully checkable.
   626	2. **The watermark** — where declaration stops: a function's signature plus its
   627	   contract. Below the mark lies the body. The mark is per-function, and movable in one
   628	   direction only: `mode: synth` lets the model write the body *down from* the mark,
   629	   with the check pipeline holding the line (D8, §5.7).
   630	3. **The floor** — the imperative interior of Rust that no grammar will ever express:
   631	   algorithms, loop bodies, data-structure manipulation. Ply *verifies* below the
   632	   watermark; it never *specifies* there.
   633	
   634	A grammar extension may push the watermark lower — express more declaratively — only if
   635	it stays visually depictable (§7.1) and clearly above the floor. Attempting to specify
   636	the floor itself means building a verification language, the abandoned path this project
   637	exists to avoid (§1).
   638	
   639	Honesty note: today the declarative stratum expresses exactly two altitudes —
   640	architecture claims and whole-function contracts. The strata between them (state
   641	machines, temporal rules, cross-function queries) are not yet expressible, and this
   642	section must not be read as implying they are. They are candidate watermark-lowering
   643	extensions, admitted one at a time through the §7.1 gate.
   644	
   645	The strata make the system's three kinds of *unspecified* distinct. The floor is
   646	permanently unspecifiable, by design. Below the watermark nothing is owed either: the
   647	body is not declared, it is verified against the contract above it. An `unresolved!`
   648	marker (§5.6) is different in kind: specification that is **owed but missing** — a
   649	tracked, numbered hole in the declarative stratum, expected to close. It sits physically
   650	in a body, but the missing decision usually belongs above the mark (the answer becomes a
   651	contract clause, an example, or a branch condition). This is why §5.6 caps such a fn at
   652	check `test`: with a decision unresolved, the contract cannot be complete, so the
   653	watermark is not yet a promise worth proving against.
   654

   659	Stability rule: additive changes only after M3; the goldens in tests/ui are the contract.
