   694	
   695	## 9. Testing strategy
   696	
   697	- **Fixture e2e**: each feature ships a minimal fixture project + `ply.yaml` + expected
   698	  `--json` output as insta goldens. Review goldens like API diffs.
   699	- **Cex validity oracle**: every rendered `cargo_test` must FAIL under `cargo test` in
   700	  the fixture before the fix and PASS after; every `kani_playback` artifact must
   701	  reproduce under `cargo kani playback` with the pinned version. The e2e harness asserts
   702	  both. A counterexample that does not reproduce is `X0902` (an adapter bug). This is
   703	  the primary correctness check of the whole tool.
   704	- **Schema goldens**: `schema/ply.schema.json` is golden-tested; a fixture set of valid
   705	  and invalid `ply.yaml` documents pins validation behavior and E0201 pointer paths.
   706	- **Extraction differential**: property-test call-graph extraction against a naive
   707	  AST-walk reference on generated fixture modules; any disagreement is a bug. Assert
   708	  resolution coverage (D11) on fixtures with known-unresolvable sites.
   709	- **Engine-absence matrix**: run every e2e once per engine with that engine masked out,
   710	  asserting graceful downgrade (`W0110` / `engine-missing`), not failure.
   711	- **Self-hosting**: golden tests for `skill` output, and `cargo ply check` runs clean on
   712	  this repo (this workspace gets its own `ply.yaml` from M2 onward, kept green in CI).
   713	  Once M5 lands, `cargo ply verify` runs over this workspace too: contracts on our own
   714	  core functions (config merge, anchor resolution, verdict aggregation), verified by our
   715	  own pipeline, in CI.
   716	
   717	## 10. Milestones (accretive; every prior acceptance test stays green)
   718	
   719	**M0 — feasibility spike** (~2 sessions; nothing else starts until ADR-0003 exists)
   720	- One throwaway fixture crate; Kani version pinned. Exercise: a contracted private free
   721	  function; a contracted method; a user-defined input type; a same-crate stubbed callee;
   722	  a cross-crate callee (expect failure — document it); cargo-mutants with a custom test
   723	  command running a generated harness; a real contract violation with
   724	  playback replay; one attempt at rendering that cex as a plain `#[test]`; the
   725	  `cfg_attr` emission from a prototype `ply-attrs`; the in-crate proof-module mechanism.
   726	- Accept: ADR-0003 records, per item, works / fails / needs-flag, and lists every spec
   727	  amendment that follows. The fixture and a script to re-run it are kept under
   728	  `tests/spike/`.
   729	
   730	**M1 — schema + model + anchors** (~3 sessions)
   731	- `schema/ply.schema.json`; serde model; validation with pointer→line mapping;
   732	  multi-file merge; micro-syntax parsers; E02xx suite (goldens). ADR-0002 written.
   733	- syn-based item index; anchor resolution with suggestions (E0301); fingerprint
   734	  recording and staleness (W0302, D14 — engine fields filled as engines land); `accept`;
   735	  `check` and `worklist` (markers only); `--json` envelope everywhere.
   736	- Accept: a fixture with 2 crates, nested components, one broken anchor, and one stale
   737	  claim (then blessed via `accept`) — golden output exact; invalid-yaml fixture set
   738	  pinned.
   739	
   740	**M2 — architecture engine** (~4 sessions)
   741	- Crate tier from `cargo metadata` (A0401/A0405 as errors). Item tier behind
   742	  `trait Extractor`: calls, caps via capmap, mutates, unresolved-call accounting;
   743	  warnings by default, `strict` upgrade; W0411/W0412; profile bans with allow-escapes;
   744	  `audit` (escapes only at this stage). ADR-0001 written.
   745	- Self-host: this workspace gets its own `ply.yaml`, checked in CI.
   746	- Accept: a fixture with a crate-tier violation (error), an item-tier cross-boundary
   747	  call (warning, then error under `strict`), a pure violation, an owns violation, and a
   748	  profile escape — goldens for each; a clean fixture producing zero diagnostics; a
   749	  fixture with a deliberately unresolvable call asserting the coverage metric and W0412.
   750	
   751	**M3 — Kani adapter + counterexample pipeline** (~8–10 sessions; shaped by ADR-0003)
   752	- ply-attrs emitting `cfg_attr(kani, ...)`; harvest of pre-existing cfg_attr-kani
   753	  attributes; `ply.yaml` contract merge; spec-subset validation (E0501); supported-
   754	  signature gate (V0505 / `unsupported`); in-crate proof-module generation for
   755	  `bounded`; run and parse Kani; playback artifacts + rendered tests where possible;
   756	  Diagnostic assembly with suggested fixes for the mechanical cases (a missing
   757	  `requires` derived from a failed side condition).
   758	- Callees-first order; `stub_verified` under D5's conditions; `conditional` verdicts
   759	  with assumption chains.
   760	- Accept: a fixture where a cex is found, its playback reproduces, and its rendered test
   761	  fails then passes, JSON golden; a two-fn fixture proving modular verification (caller
   762	  stubs proved callee; assumption chain empty); a caller-of-fuzzed-callee fixture
   763	  earning `conditional`; an unsupported-signature fixture earning `unsupported`, not a
   764	  build failure.
   765	
   766	**M4 — fuzz + test + mutate checks** (~4 sessions)
   767	- proptest harness generation over the supported-signature set (ints biased small,
   768	  structs field-by-field, Vec length 0–8; `requires` as rejection filter with a
   769	  high-rejection warning); shrink → rendered test.
   770	- `test` check: example tests + generated direct contract cases.
   771	- cargo-mutants scoped `--re` per fn with the test/fuzz kill signal (D12); E0504;
   772	  weak-spec detection (W0502) wired into `worklist`.
   773	- Accept: a seeded-bug fixture shrunk to a minimal cex; a vacuous-ensures fixture
   774	  flagged weak; a strong-spec fixture earning `·spec-strong`; a mutate-without-tier
   775	  fixture producing E0504.
   776	
   777	**M5 — verdict tree, aggregation, skill, polish** (~3 sessions)
   778	- Verdict tree with worst-of, status propagation, and open items; `--depth`/`--focus`;
   779	  `audit` completed (escapes + assumptions + derived); `skill` generation embedding the
   780	  schema; `--only-changed`.
   781	- Accept: a nested fixture where one weak leaf drags down the root verdict and a
   782	  `conditional` mid-tree node propagates as a status; tree JSON golden; skill file
   783	  golden.
   784	
   785	**M6 — synth (experimental)** (~3 sessions)
   786	- The §5.7 loop with a pluggable model command; derived marking; hand-edit detection.
   787	- Accept: a fixture where a synth fn with 2 examples + 1 ensures converges within 5
   788	  rounds against the M3/M4 pipeline. Record the transcript as a fixture artifact. The
   789	  pipeline mechanics are what's under test, so provide a `MockModel` that replays a
   790	  canned transcript, and allow marking the live-model test `#[ignore]` in CI.
   791	
   792	**M7 — Verus prove check + optional Miri** (**optional**: do not start without an ADR,
   793	informed by M3–M5 experience, arguing the translation is worth its cost; expect it to be
   794	compiler-scale work, not a thin adapter)
   795	- Translate the spec subset to Verus for a profile-restricted fragment; adapter; verdict
   796	  `proved`; document the fragment's limits honestly in SCHEMA.md.
   797	- Accept: a gcd-style fixture proved end to end; a fixture outside the fragment failing
   798	  with a clear diagnostic naming the unsupported construct.
   799	
   800	Out of scope entirely: concurrency verification (loom, weak-memory models, lock-free
   801	data structures — deliberately excluded, not forgotten; the engines here check
   802	single-threaded behavior), canvas/GUI, data-flow (`~>`) checking, LSP/editor integration,
   803	model-based two-state specs beyond `old()` (sequence histories, FIFO ordering — `old()`
   804	itself is in scope, §5.4a), the `induct` check, cross-crate `stub_verified`,
   805	proof-backed mutation, async fns in verified components, and the internals of crates
   806	outside the workspace.
   807	
     1	//! The pure verdict kernel of the future `cargo ply`: evidence levels,
     2	//! statuses, and the worst-of aggregation rule (The-Ply-Spec.md §7, D5, D6). No I/O,
     3	//! no external crates, no code that anchors to real source -- that belongs
     4	//! to `ply-model`/`ply-core` later. `ply-model`'s own doc comment already
     5	//! calls this split out explicitly ("verdicts, statuses, and fingerprints
     6	//! need anchored code and are out of scope for this crate"); this crate is
     7	//! that carve-out, kept dependency-free so its four standing invariants
     8	//! (below) can be checked exhaustively and, once Kani is installed, proved.
     9	//!
    10	//! ## Scope
    11	//!
    12	//! The-Ply-Spec.md §7 describes a full tree node as
    13	//! `{ id, kind, anchor, content_hash, verdict, statuses, worst_descendant,
    14	//! open_items }`. This kernel models only the part that is pure computation
    15	//! over already-known verdicts: a node's own claim ([`NodeKind`]), its own
    16	//! [`StatusKind`] set and conditional assumptions, plus [`aggregate`], which
    17	//! computes `worst_descendant`/`statuses`/`open_items` from a tree of such
    18	//! nodes. `id`/`anchor`/`content_hash` are identity and provenance concerns
    19	//! that need a real codebase to anchor to; they are out of scope here and
    20	//! belong to `ply-model`/`ply-core`.
    21	//!
    22	//! The-Ply-Spec.md §7's paragraph "Aggregation rules the verdict kernel
    23	//! (`tools/kernel`) checks exhaustively" is now the normative source for the
    24	//! four rules below; earlier revisions of this file carried conservative
    25	//! readings for two of them (own-evidence-for-containers, and
    26	//! conditional/assumptions pairing) that the amendment has since settled --
    27	//! see the doc comments on [`NodeKind`] and [`VerdictNode::conditional`].
    28	
    29	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
    30	
    31	/// The-Ply-Spec.md D6 / §7: "The evidence order compares the six kinds" --
    32	/// `violation < unclaimed < tested < fuzzed < bounded < proved`. Declaration
    33	/// order below *is* that order, so `#[derive(PartialOrd, Ord)]` gives the
    34	/// comparison for free -- "worst" is simply the smaller value.
    35	///
    36	/// Conservative reading (spec silent): §7 also says the `n`/`k` parameters of
    37	/// `fuzzed(n)`/`bounded(k)` are "reported in the verdict, never compared" --
    38	/// two claims of the same kind are the same rung regardless of parameter.
    39	/// The kernel therefore compares kinds only and carries no n/k payload; a
    40	/// parameter-aware tie-break, if ever wanted, is a model-layer concern built
    41	/// on top of this order, not a change to it.
    42	///
    43	/// `#[cfg_attr(kani, derive(kani::Arbitrary))]` mirrors The-Ply-Spec.md D2's own
    44	/// idiom (attributes that vanish under plain cargo and activate only under
    45	/// `cargo kani`) so the Kani harnesses below can draw real `Evidence` values
    46	/// via `kani::any()` instead of a hand-rolled index-to-variant mapping.
    47	#[cfg_attr(kani, derive(kani::Arbitrary))]
    48	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    49	pub enum Evidence {
    50	    Violation,
    51	    Unclaimed,
    52	    Tested,
    53	    Fuzzed,
    54	    Bounded,
    55	    Proved,
    56	}
    57	
    58	/// The-Ply-Spec.md §7: "Only claimable items contribute their own evidence. fns fold
    59	/// their own verdict into `worst_descendant`; containers (workspace,
    60	/// components) fold over children only."
    61	///
    62	/// This is a node-kind distinction rather than a sentinel `Evidence` value
    63	/// so a container is unable to carry its own evidence *by construction*: a
    64	/// `Container` value simply has no `Evidence` payload to set, so there is no
    65	/// representable state for "a component claims its own verdict" to be
    66	/// mistakenly read as one -- the compiler rules it out, not a convention.
    67	#[cfg_attr(kani, derive(kani::Arbitrary))]
    68	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    69	pub enum NodeKind {
    70	    /// A claimable item (a fn claim, in the real model) with its own
    71	    /// evidence.
    72	    Claimable(Evidence),
    73	    /// workspace or component: no claim of its own, ever.
    74	    Container,
    75	}
    76	
    77	/// The-Ply-Spec.md §0 / §7: "Statuses ... do not sit in that [evidence] order; they
    78	/// propagate upward as flags and open-item counts alongside it." This is the
    79	/// status vocabulary named in §0's glossary row for `status`, minus
    80	/// `conditional` -- see [`VerdictNode::conditional`] for why that one moved
    81	/// out of this set.
    82	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    83	pub enum StatusKind {
    84	    Stale,
    85	    WeakSpec,
    86	    Unsupported,
    87	    EngineMissing,
    88	    Timeout,
    89	    Inconclusive,
    90	}
    91	
    92	/// One node of a verdict tree (The-Ply-Spec.md §7), reduced to exactly what
    93	/// [`aggregate`] needs: this node's own claim ([`NodeKind`]), its own status
    94	/// flags, its own conditional assumptions (if any), and its children.
    95	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    96	pub struct VerdictNode {
    97	    pub kind: NodeKind,
    98	    pub statuses: BTreeSet<StatusKind>,
    99	    /// The-Ply-Spec.md §7 / D5: "`conditional` structurally carries its assumptions:
   100	    /// a conditional status without an assumptions list is unrepresentable
   101	    /// in the kernel, not validated against." `None` = not conditional;
   102	    /// `Some(assumptions)` = conditional, with exactly the assumptions it
   103	    /// rests on riding along in the same value -- there is no way to
   104	    /// construct "conditional" without also supplying the list, because
   105	    /// they are the same field rather than a flag plus an independent one
   106	    /// that could disagree with it.
   107	    pub conditional: Option<Vec<String>>,
   108	    pub children: Vec<VerdictNode>,
   109	}
   110	
   111	/// The result of [`aggregate`] at one tree position: this node's subtree
   112	/// (itself plus every descendant) folded into `worst_descendant`, unioned
   113	/// `statuses`, unioned conditional assumptions, and a total `open_items`
   114	/// count. Mirrors the input tree's shape via `children`.
   115	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
   116	pub struct AggregatedNode {
   117	    /// The-Ply-Spec.md §7's `worst_descendant`: the worst-of (D6) over every
   118	    /// *claimable* node's own evidence in the subtree. `Evidence::Unclaimed`
   119	    /// exactly when the subtree contains no claimable node at all (§7: "A
   120	    /// container with no claimable descendants reads `unclaimed`").
   121	    pub evidence: Evidence,
   122	    /// Union of this node's own `statuses` and every descendant's own
   123	    /// `statuses` -- D6/§7: "statuses ... propagate upward as flags."
   124	    pub statuses: BTreeSet<StatusKind>,
   125	    /// Sorted, deduplicated union of the assumptions carried by every node
   126	    /// in the subtree (self included) whose own [`VerdictNode::conditional`]
   127	    /// is `Some(_)`. `None` exactly when no node in the subtree is
   128	    /// conditional -- this is standing obligation 2: a conditional status
   129	    /// never reaches an ancestor without the assumptions that justify it.
   130	    pub conditional: Option<Vec<String>>,
   131	    /// The-Ply-Spec.md §7's `open_items`, folded as a count: "`open_items` counts
   132	    /// flag instances, not flagged nodes: a node carrying two statuses
   133	    /// contributes 2." This kernel counts every `StatusKind` flag plus a
   134	    /// `conditional` (when present) as one flag instance each, summed over
   135	    /// every node in the subtree. Unresolved markers (`ply.yaml` registry
   136	    /// entries, §5.6) have no representation in this kernel's node type --
   137	    /// they anchor to code the kernel never sees -- so they are not part of
   138	    /// this count; a model layer that also tracks them would add its own
   139	    /// count to this one.
   140	    pub open_items: usize,
   141	    pub children: Vec<AggregatedNode>,
   142	}
   143	
   144	fn merge_conditional(a: Option<Vec<String>>, b: Option<Vec<String>>) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
   145	    match (a, b) {
   146	        (None, None) => None,
   147	        (Some(v), None) | (None, Some(v)) => Some(v),
   148	        (Some(mut v), Some(w)) => {
   149	            v.extend(w);
   150	            Some(v)
   151	        }
   152	    }
   153	}
   154	
   155	/// Combines two "min over claimable-only evidence so far" accumulators,
   156	/// where `None` means "no claimable node found yet" rather than any real
   157	/// evidence value. Not-yet-found must be a true no-op here, never folded in
   158	/// through `Evidence::min` as if `Unclaimed` were a placeholder identity:
   159	/// `Unclaimed` is a real, comparable rung (weaker than `Tested` but
   160	/// stronger than `Violation`), so treating "nothing here" as literal
   161	/// `Unclaimed` and folding it via `.min()` would let an empty container
   162	/// wrongly drag down a genuinely stronger claimable sibling or child. (An
   163	/// earlier version of `aggregate` did exactly this -- see the note on
   164	/// `aggregate_raw` below.)
   165	fn combine_claimable(a: Option<Evidence>, b: Option<Evidence>) -> Option<Evidence> {
   166	    match (a, b) {
   167	        (None, x) => x,
   168	        (x, None) => x,
   169	        (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)),
   170	    }
   171	}
   172	
   173	/// The recursive core of [`aggregate`]. Returns the subtree's "min over
   174	/// claimable-only evidence" as an `Option<Evidence>` (`None` = no claimable
   175	/// node anywhere in the subtree) *alongside* the public [`AggregatedNode`]
   176	/// for this position, whose own `evidence` field is that option already
   177	/// defaulted to `Evidence::Unclaimed` for display (The-Ply-Spec.md §7: "A container
   178	/// with no claimable descendants reads `unclaimed`").
   179	///
   180	/// The two-value return exists because those are genuinely different
   210	        open_items += child_agg.open_items;
   211	        children.push(child_agg);
   212	    }
   213	
   214	    if let Some(c) = &mut conditional {
   215	        c.sort();
   216	        c.dedup();
   217	    }
   218	
   219	    let evidence = raw_evidence.unwrap_or(Evidence::Unclaimed);
   220	    (
   221	        raw_evidence,
   222	        AggregatedNode {
   223	            evidence,
   224	            statuses,
   225	            conditional,
   226	            open_items,
   227	            children,
   228	        },
   229	    )
   230	}
   231	
   232	/// Fold a verdict tree into its per-node aggregated results (The-Ply-Spec.md §7,
   233	/// D6). Pure: no I/O, no randomness, no shared mutable state -- calling it
   234	/// twice on equal inputs always yields equal outputs (standing obligation
   235	/// 4). Every collection used for aggregated state (`BTreeSet`, plus a
   236	/// sorted+deduplicated `Vec` for conditional assumptions) is
   237	/// order-independent by construction, specifically to avoid the classic
   238	/// footgun where a hash-based set's iteration order can differ between two
   239	/// otherwise-equal instances (Rust's `HashSet`/`HashMap` seed their hasher
   240	/// per instance) -- that would make two `AggregatedNode` values compare
   241	/// unequal, or print differently, for no reason tied to the data itself.
   242	pub fn aggregate(node: &VerdictNode) -> AggregatedNode {
   243	    aggregate_raw(node).1
   244	}
   245	
   246	#[cfg(test)]
   247	mod tests {
   248	    use super::*;
   249	
   250	    fn leaf(kind: NodeKind) -> VerdictNode {
   251	        VerdictNode {
   252	            kind,
   253	            statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   254	            conditional: None,
   255	            children: Vec::new(),
   256	        }
   257	    }
   258	
   259	    #[test]
   260	    fn evidence_order_matches_d6() {
   261	        assert!(Evidence::Violation < Evidence::Unclaimed);
   262	        assert!(Evidence::Unclaimed < Evidence::Tested);
   263	        assert!(Evidence::Tested < Evidence::Fuzzed);
   264	        assert!(Evidence::Fuzzed < Evidence::Bounded);
   265	        assert!(Evidence::Bounded < Evidence::Proved);
   266	    }
   267	
   268	    #[test]
   269	    fn a_single_claimable_leaf_aggregates_to_its_own_evidence() {
   270	        let agg = aggregate(&leaf(NodeKind::Claimable(Evidence::Proved)));
   271	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, Evidence::Proved);
   272	        assert!(agg.statuses.is_empty());
   273	        assert!(agg.conditional.is_none());
   274	        assert_eq!(agg.open_items, 0);
   275	    }
   276	
   277	    #[test]
   278	    fn an_empty_container_aggregates_to_unclaimed() {
   279	        let agg = aggregate(&leaf(NodeKind::Container));
   280	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, Evidence::Unclaimed);
   420	                    (x, None) => x,
   421	                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)),
   422	                };
   423	            }
   424	            if self.has_child_b {
   425	                acc = match (acc, self.child_b.own_evidence()) {
   426	                    (None, x) => x,
   427	                    (x, None) => x,
   428	                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)),
   429	                };
   430	            }
   431	            acc
   432	        }
   433	    }
   434	
   435	    /// Standing obligation 1 (as reworded by The-Ply-Spec.md §7's amendment):
   436	    /// worst-of never reports evidence stronger than the weakest claimable
   437	    /// node in the subtree, and reports exactly `Unclaimed` when there is
   438	    /// none. Standing obligation 3 (violation-reaches-root) is the case of
   439	    /// this where the weakest claimable node is a `Violation`.
   440	    #[kani::proof]
   441	    fn proof_worst_of_evidence() {
   442	        let t: SymTree = kani::any();
   443	        let expected = t.expected_claimable_min().unwrap_or(Evidence::Unclaimed);
   444	
   445	        let agg = aggregate(&t.build());
   446	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, expected);
   447	
   448	        let any_violation = t.root.own_evidence() == Some(Evidence::Violation)
   449	            || (t.has_child_a && t.child_a.own_evidence() == Some(Evidence::Violation))
   450	            || (t.has_child_b && t.child_b.own_evidence() == Some(Evidence::Violation));
   451	        if any_violation {
   452	            assert_eq!(agg.evidence, Evidence::Violation);
   453	        }
   454	    }
   455	
   456	    /// Standing obligation 2: `conditional` never disappears without its
   457	    /// assumptions.
   458	    #[kani::proof]
   459	    fn proof_conditional_carries_its_assumptions() {
   460	        let t: SymTree = kani::any();
   461	        let any_conditional = t.root.conditional
   462	            || (t.has_child_a && t.child_a.conditional)
   463	            || (t.has_child_b && t.child_b.conditional);
   464	
   465	        let agg = aggregate(&t.build());
   466	        assert_eq!(agg.conditional.is_some(), any_conditional);
   467	        if any_conditional {
   468	            assert!(!agg.conditional.unwrap().is_empty());
   469	        }
   470	    }
   471	
   472	    /// Standing obligation 4: aggregating the same tree twice yields
   473	    /// identical results.
   474	    #[kani::proof]
   475	    fn proof_aggregate_is_deterministic() {
   476	        let t: SymTree = kani::any();
   477	        let a = aggregate(&t.build());
   478	        let b = aggregate(&t.build());
   479	        assert_eq!(a, b);
   480	    }
   481	
   482	    // --- TEMPORARY diagnostic harnesses, to be removed after timing. ---
   483	
   484	    #[kani::proof]
   485	    fn tmp_single_leaf_no_status() {
   486	        let e: Evidence = kani::any();
   487	        let node = VerdictNode {
   488	            kind: NodeKind::Claimable(e),
   489	            statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   490	            conditional: None,
   491	            children: Vec::new(),
   492	        };
   493	        let agg = aggregate(&node);
   494	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, e);
   495	    }
   496	
   497	    #[kani::proof]
   498	    fn tmp_single_leaf_with_status() {
   499	        let leaf: SymLeaf = kani::any();
   500	        let node = leaf.into_node(Vec::new());
   501	        let agg = aggregate(&node);
   502	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, leaf.own_evidence().unwrap_or(Evidence::Unclaimed));
   503	    }
   504	
   505	    #[kani::proof]
   506	    fn tmp_container_one_child_no_status() {
   507	        let root_kind: NodeKind = kani::any();
   508	        let a: Evidence = kani::any();
   509	        let node = VerdictNode {
   510	            kind: root_kind,
   511	            statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   512	            conditional: None,
   513	            children: vec![VerdictNode {
   514	                kind: NodeKind::Claimable(a),
   515	                statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   516	                conditional: None,
   517	                children: Vec::new(),
   518	            }],
   519	        };
   520	        let _agg = aggregate(&node);
   521	    }
   522	
   523	    #[kani::proof]
   524	    fn tmp_container_two_children_no_status() {
   525	        let root_kind: NodeKind = kani::any();
   526	        let a: Evidence = kani::any();
   527	        let b: Evidence = kani::any();
   528	        let node = VerdictNode {
   529	            kind: root_kind,
   530	            statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   531	            conditional: None,
   532	            children: vec![
   533	                VerdictNode { kind: NodeKind::Claimable(a), statuses: BTreeSet::new(), conditional: None, children: Vec::new() },
   534	                VerdictNode { kind: NodeKind::Claimable(b), statuses: BTreeSet::new(), conditional: None, children: Vec::new() },
   535	            ],
   536	        };
   537	        let _agg = aggregate(&node);
   538	    }
   539	}
     1	//! Document-local `ply.yaml` validation (The-Ply-Spec.md §5.1a, §5.1, §5.6) — the
     2	//! subset of `cargo ply check` (§6) that needs no anchored Rust code:
     3	//! unknown fields, schema shape, and required fields are already rejected
     4	//! by `ply_model::parse_document`'s strict serde parse (nothing to
     5	//! duplicate here). This crate only flags constructs that parse cleanly but
     6	//! still violate The-Ply-Spec.md. Anchor resolution, staleness, and the
     7	//! architecture rules (§5.2, §5.3) need real code behind the anchors and
     8	//! are out of scope.
     9	
    10	use ply_model::{Check, Component, Document, Edge, parse_check, parse_deny, parse_edge};
    11	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    12	
    13	/// Where a diagnostic attaches for drawing (The-Ply-Spec.md §7.1 "finding" row).
    14	/// `ply-render` consumes this to know what to mark red; `ply-check`'s own
    15	/// stdout/exit-code contract never prints it (see `Diagnostic`'s `Display`).
    16	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    17	pub enum Target {
    18	    /// A fn claim, named by its component's fully qualified dotted path
    19	    /// (top-level component name, or `parent.child` for nested ones) plus
    20	    /// its own fn key.
    21	    Fn {
    22	        component_path: String,
    23	        fn_name: String,
    24	    },
    25	    /// A component (its box), named by the same qualified path.
    26	    Component(String),
    27	    /// An entry in `doc.edges`, by position.
    28	    EdgeIndex(usize),
    29	    /// An entry in `doc.deny`, by position.
    30	    DenyIndex(usize),
    31	    /// An unresolved marker, by its declared id — may attach to more than
    32	    /// one drawn pin if that id is a duplicate (that's the finding).
    33	    UnresolvedId(u64),
    34	    /// No single item in the document is the offender; the renderer falls
    35	    /// back to a count next to the workspace title.
    36	    Document,
    37	}
    38	
    39	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    40	pub struct Diagnostic {
    41	    pub code: &'static str,
    42	    pub message: String,
    43	    pub target: Target,
    44	}
    45	
    46	impl std::fmt::Display for Diagnostic {
    47	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    48	        write!(f, "{}: {}", self.code, self.message)
    49	    }
    50	}
    51	
    52	impl Diagnostic {
    53	    /// Severity, derived from the code prefix (The-Ply-Spec.md §5.3: item-tier
    54	    /// rules are `W`-severity by default). A `W`-code is advisory — `ply
    55	    /// check` reports it, but it must not by itself fail the run; every other
    56	    /// code this crate emits is a document-local error.
    57	    pub fn is_advisory(&self) -> bool {
    58	        self.code.starts_with('W')
    59	    }
    60	}
    61	
    62	fn diag(code: &'static str, message: String, target: Target) -> Diagnostic {
    63	    Diagnostic {
    64	        code,
    65	        message,
    66	        target,
    67	    }
    68	}
    69	
    70	/// §5.1a rule 3: anchors and fn keys are plain segment paths —
    71	/// `IDENT(::IDENT)*`, where a segment may also be a type name in
    72	/// `Type::method` position. No generics, no trait-qualified paths, no
    73	/// lifetimes — anything else is `E0304 unsupported path form`.
    74	fn is_valid_path_form(s: &str) -> bool {
    75	    !s.is_empty()
    76	        && s.split("::").all(|seg| {
    77	            let mut chars = seg.chars();
    78	            matches!(chars.next(), Some(c) if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_')
    79	                && chars.all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_')
    80	        })
    81	}
    82	
    83	/// §5.1 / §5.1a rule 4: every check string must parse under the real
    84	/// `test | fuzz(N)? | bounded(K)? | prove | mutate` micro-syntax, numeric
    85	/// bounds included. A string serde accepted as a plain `String` but that
    86	/// fails this parser is `E0203`.
    87	fn check_syntax(checks: &[String], location: &str, target: &Target, out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    88	    for c in checks {
    89	        if let Err(e) = parse_check(c) {
    90	            out.push(diag("E0203", format!("{e} ({location})"), target.clone()));
    91	        }
    92	    }
    93	}
    94	
    95	/// §5.1: `mutate` without a `test` or `fuzz` entry in the *same* checks list
    96	/// is `E0504`. Checked independently wherever a checks list appears
    97	/// (a component's default checks, or a fn claim's own) — this rule does
    98	/// not merge inherited and explicit checks, since that merge isn't spelled
    99	/// out anywhere as part of the document-local grammar.
   100	fn check_mutate_rule(
   101	    checks: &[String],
   102	    location: &str,
   103	    target: &Target,
   104	    out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>,
   105	) {
   106	    let mut has_mutate = false;
   107	    let mut has_test_or_fuzz = false;
   108	    for c in checks {
   109	        match parse_check(c) {
   110	            Ok(Check::Mutate) => has_mutate = true,
   111	            Ok(Check::Test) | Ok(Check::Fuzz(_)) => has_test_or_fuzz = true,
   112	            _ => {}
   113	        }
   114	    }
   115	    if has_mutate && !has_test_or_fuzz {
   116	        out.push(diag(
   117	            "E0504",
   118	            format!(
   119	                "mutate has nothing to catch its planted bugs: add a test or fuzz check \
   120	                 beside it — mutation testing works by deliberately breaking the code and \
   260	        return all_qualified.contains(token).then(|| token.to_string());
   261	    }
   262	    match leaf_index.get(token) {
   263	        Some(paths) if paths.len() == 1 => Some(paths[0].clone()),
   264	        _ => None,
   265	    }
   266	}
   267	
   268	/// True if `other` is `ancestor` itself plus at least one more dotted
   269	/// segment — i.e. `ancestor` is a strict prefix of `other` ending on a `.`
   270	/// boundary, matching how nested qualified paths are built in
   271	/// `walk_component`.
   272	fn is_strict_ancestor(ancestor: &str, other: &str) -> bool {
   273	    other.len() > ancestor.len()
   274	        && other.starts_with(ancestor)
   275	        && other.as_bytes()[ancestor.len()] == b'.'
   276	}
   277	
   278	/// §5.3: an edge whose two endpoints lie on one nesting line — a component
   279	/// and its own descendant, either direction, at any depth — is redundant:
   280	/// containment already grants the permission the edge would declare.
   281	/// Applies to both edge kinds (`->` and `~>`); the spec paragraph states the
   282	/// rule in terms of "an explicit edge" and closes with "Edges are for
   283	/// crossings between nesting lines", neither qualified to calls only.
   284	fn check_containment_redundancy(
   285	    edge_str: &str,
   286	    edge: &Edge,
   287	    leaf_index: &HashMap<String, Vec<String>>,
   288	    all_qualified: &HashSet<String>,
   289	    target: &Target,
   290	    out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>,
   291	) {
   292	    let Some(from) = resolve_component_ref(&edge.from, leaf_index, all_qualified) else {
   293	        return;
   294	    };
   295	    let Some(to) = resolve_component_ref(&edge.to, leaf_index, all_qualified) else {
   296	        return;
   297	    };
   298	
   299	    let (outer, inner) = if is_strict_ancestor(&from, &to) {
   300	        (from, to)
   301	    } else if is_strict_ancestor(&to, &from) {
   302	        (to, from)
   303	    } else {
   304	        return;
   305	    };
   306	
   307	    out.push(diag(
   308	        "W0409",
   309	        format!(
   310	            "\"edge {}\" is redundant: {inner} is inside {outer}, and a component may always \
   311	             call within its own nesting line — no edge needed",
   312	            edge_str.trim(),
   313	        ),
   314	        target.clone(),
   315	    ));
   316	}
   317	
   318	pub fn run_checks(doc: &Document) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
   319	    let mut out = Vec::new();
   320	    let mut unresolved_ids: Vec<(u64, String)> = Vec::new();
   321	    let mut leaf_index: HashMap<String, Vec<String>> = HashMap::new();
   322	
   323	    for (name, c) in &doc.components {
   324	        walk_component(
   325	            name,
   326	            name,
   327	            c,
   328	            &mut out,
   329	            &mut unresolved_ids,
   330	            &mut leaf_index,
   331	        );
   332	    }
   333	
   334	    // Every qualified path any component actually has, used to resolve a
   335	    // dotted edge endpoint literally (§5.1a rule 6's dotted form) without
   336	    // re-deriving it from layout.
   337	    let all_qualified: HashSet<String> = leaf_index.values().flatten().cloned().collect();
   338	
   339	    for (i, e) in doc.edges.iter().enumerate() {
   340	        let target = Target::EdgeIndex(i);
   341	        match parse_edge(e) {
   342	            Ok(edge) => {
   343	                check_token_ambiguity(&edge.from, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   344	                check_token_ambiguity(&edge.to, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   345	                check_containment_redundancy(
   346	                    e,
   347	                    &edge,
   348	                    &leaf_index,
   349	                    &all_qualified,
   350	                    &target,
   351	                    &mut out,
   352	                );
   353	            }
   354	            Err(err) => out.push(diag("E0203", format!("{err} (edges)"), target)),
   355	        }
   356	    }
   357	    for (i, d) in doc.deny.iter().enumerate() {
   358	        let target = Target::DenyIndex(i);
   359	        match parse_deny(d) {
   360	            Ok(deny) => {
   361	                check_token_ambiguity(&deny.from, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   362	                check_token_ambiguity(&deny.to, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   363	            }
   364	            Err(err) => out.push(diag("E0203", format!("{err} (deny)"), target)),
   365	        }
   366	    }
   367	
   368	    for entry in &doc.unresolved {
   369	        unresolved_ids.push((entry.id, "registry".to_string()));
   370	    }
   371	    // §5.1a rule 5: unresolved ids are unique across the whole merged
   372	    // workspace, registry and fn entries together. Report in encounter
   373	    // order (component walk, then top-level registry) so output is
   374	    // deterministic without needing a stable-sorted map.
   375	    let mut first_seen: HashMap<u64, String> = HashMap::new();
   376	    for (id, location) in &unresolved_ids {
   377	        match first_seen.get(id) {
   378	            Some(prev) => out.push(diag(
   379	                "E0205",
   380	                format!(
   381	                    "unresolved id {id} is used twice ({prev} and {location}): each open \
   382	                     decision needs its own number"
   383	                ),
   384	                Target::UnresolvedId(*id),
   385	            )),
   386	            None => {
   387	                first_seen.insert(*id, location.clone());
   388	            }
   389	        }
   390	    }
    90	/// Clear space kept between an any-node, the line it anchors, and whatever
    91	/// sits next to it (the frame edge on one side, a real component box or an
    92	/// `except` label on the other).
    93	const ANY_GAP: f64 = 16.0;
    94	
    95	/// Every `class` this renderer emits must have a rule here. SVG's initial
    96	/// paint is `fill: black; stroke: none`, so an unstyled shape is a solid black
    97	/// box — a missing rule is invisible output, not a cosmetic slip.
    98	/// `tests/render.rs::every_painted_element_resolves_a_style_rule` enforces it.
    99	pub const STYLE: &str = "\
   100	.workspace-frame{fill:#fbfbfd;stroke:#c8ccd4}\
   101	.workspace-title{fill:#6b7280}\
   102	.component-box{stroke:#3b4252;stroke-width:1.5}\
   103	.hollow-box{stroke-dasharray:6 4}\
   104	.collapsed-stack{stroke:#3b4252;stroke-width:1.5}\
   105	.pure-seal{fill:none;stroke:#3b4252}\
   106	.component-name{fill:#1f2430;font-weight:bold}\
   107	.component-anchor{fill:#6b7280;font-size:10px}\
   108	.component-owns{fill:#6b7280;font-size:10px;font-style:italic}\
   109	.ceiling-unclaimed{fill:#fff}\
   110	.ceiling-tested{fill:#eaf6ec}\
   111	.ceiling-fuzzed{fill:#cdeed3}\
   112	.ceiling-bounded{fill:#a3e0b3}\
   113	.ceiling-proved{fill:#78d194}\
   114	.contract-mark{fill:#1f2430}\
   115	.cap-badge rect{fill:#fdecec;stroke:#c9534f}\
   116	.cap-badge text{fill:#8f2f2c;font-size:10px}\
   117	.profile-tag rect{fill:#eef2fb;stroke:#5570a8}\
   118	.profile-tag text{fill:#334b78;font-size:10px}\
   119	.fn-chip-box{fill:#f6f7f9;stroke:#9aa2b1}\
   120	.fn-name{fill:#1f2430}\
   121	.fn-checks{fill:#2f6f4f;font-size:11px}\
   122	.fn-check-with{fill:#6b7280;font-size:10px}\
   123	.fn-shield{fill:none;stroke:#9a7a1f;font-size:13px}\
   124	.unresolved-pin circle,.registry-pin circle{fill:#fff6d8;stroke:#b08900}\
   125	.pin-label{fill:#7a5c00;font-size:10px;text-anchor:middle}\
   126	.edge-line{fill:none;stroke:#3b4252;stroke-width:1.5}\
   127	.edge-label{fill:#3b4252;font-size:10px;text-anchor:middle}\
   128	.deny-line{fill:none;stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:1.5}\
   129	.deny-bar{stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:3}\
   130	.deny-except{fill:#8f2f2c;font-size:10px;text-anchor:middle}\
   131	.any-node circle{fill:#eceef2;stroke:#9aa2b1}\
   132	.any-label{fill:#4b5563;text-anchor:middle}\
   133	#arrow path{fill:#3b4252}\
   134	";
   135	
   136	/// Rules for the §7.1 "finding" classes, kept separate from `STYLE` and
   137	/// appended to the embedded `<style>` only on a document that actually has
   138	/// at least one finding (`render_svg`'s `style` local). A clean document —
   139	/// every vetting fixture, the golden snapshot — must render byte-identical
   140	/// to before this feature existed; folding these rules permanently into
   141	/// `STYLE` would grow *every* document's stylesheet text regardless of
   142	/// whether it uses any of these classes, which is exactly the kind of leak
   143	/// CLAUDE.md's golden-review rule exists to catch.
   144	pub const FINDING_STYLE: &str = "\
   145	.fn-chip-box-finding{fill:#fdecec;stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:2.5}\
   146	.component-box-finding{stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:3}\
   147	.edge-line-finding{fill:none;stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:3}\
   148	.deny-line-finding{fill:none;stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:3}\
   149	.unresolved-pin-finding circle,.registry-pin-finding circle{fill:#fdecec;stroke:#c9534f;stroke-width:2.5}\
   150	.pin-label-finding{fill:#8f2f2c;font-size:10px;text-anchor:middle}\
   151	.finding-badge rect{fill:#c9534f;stroke:#8f2f2c}\
   152	.finding-badge text{fill:#fff;font-size:9px;font-weight:bold;text-anchor:middle}\
   153	.finding-count{fill:#c9534f;font-size:11px;font-weight:bold}\
   154	";
   155	
   420	
   421	// ---- §7.1 "declared ceiling" -----------------------------------------------
   422	//
   423	// A promise, not proof: the strongest verdict a component's *declared*
   424	// checks could earn if they all passed, computed the same way a real
   425	// verdict tree would be (worst-of over fns, folded by the kernel's own
   426	// container rule) but from declared check *kinds* rather than run results.
   427	
   428	/// The strongest check kind one fn declares (The-Ply-Spec.md §7.1: `test` ->
   429	/// tested, `fuzz(n)` -> fuzzed, `bounded(k)` -> bounded, `prove` -> proved).
   430	/// `mutate` strengthens nothing on its own (it only ever rides alongside a
   431	/// `test`/`fuzz` entry, D12) and an unparseable string names no real kind, so
   432	/// both are skipped rather than treated as evidence. No checks at all (or
   433	/// only skipped ones) -> `Unclaimed`.
   434	fn fn_declared_ceiling(fc: &FnClaim) -> Evidence {
   435	    let mut best: Option<Evidence> = None;
   436	    for c in &fc.checks {
   437	        let kind = match parse_check(c) {
   438	            Ok(Check::Test) => Some(Evidence::Tested),
   439	            Ok(Check::Fuzz(_)) => Some(Evidence::Fuzzed),
   440	            Ok(Check::Bounded(_)) => Some(Evidence::Bounded),
   441	            Ok(Check::Prove) => Some(Evidence::Proved),
   442	            Ok(Check::Mutate) | Err(_) => None,
   443	        };
   444	        if let Some(k) = kind {
   445	            best = Some(best.map_or(k, |b: Evidence| b.max(k)));
   446	        }
   447	    }
   448	    best.unwrap_or(Evidence::Unclaimed)
   449	}
   450	
   451	/// Builds this component's subtree as real `ply_kernel::VerdictNode`s: its
   452	/// own fns as `Claimable` leaves (never `Violation` — a declared ceiling is
   453	/// never earned evidence, so that rung is unreachable here), its nested
   454	/// components recursively as `Container`s. Handed to the kernel's own
   455	/// `aggregate` — never re-folded by hand — so the worst-of rule this draws
   456	/// is the exact one The-Ply-Spec.md §7 pins and `tools/kernel` checks
   457	/// exhaustively.
   458	fn component_verdict_node(comp: &Component) -> VerdictNode {
   459	    let mut children: Vec<VerdictNode> = comp
   460	        .fns
   461	        .values()
   462	        .map(|fc| VerdictNode {
   463	            kind: NodeKind::Claimable(fn_declared_ceiling(fc)),
   464	            statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   465	            conditional: None,
   466	            children: Vec::new(),
   467	        })
   468	        .collect();
   469	    children.extend(comp.components.values().map(component_verdict_node));
   470	    VerdictNode {
   471	        kind: NodeKind::Container,
   472	        statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   473	        conditional: None,
   474	        children,
   475	    }
   476	}
   477	
   478	fn component_ceiling(comp: &Component) -> Evidence {
   479	    aggregate(&component_verdict_node(comp)).evidence
   480	}
  1080	/// really is empty.
  1081	fn render_component_dispatch(
  1082	    name: &str,
  1083	    qualified: &str,
  1084	    comp: &Component,
  1085	    walk: &WalkCtx,
  1086	    level: usize,
  1087	) -> ComponentBox {
  1088	    let is_hollow = comp.fns.is_empty() && comp.components.is_empty();
  1089	    if !is_hollow && walk.collapse.should_collapse(qualified, level) {
  1090	        render_collapsed_component(name, qualified, comp, walk.profiles, walk.findings)
  1091	    } else {
  1092	        render_component(name, qualified, comp, walk, level)
  1093	    }
  1094	}
  1095	
  1096	/// A collapsed component (The-Ply-Spec.md §7.1): one solid-bordered box
  1097	/// showing name, anchor, a `N components · M fns` contents line, and the
  1098	/// same worst-descendant ceiling fill an expanded box would earn (computed
  1099	/// over the *full* subtree, via `component_ceiling`, exactly as an expanded
  1100	/// box's fill already is). Three things never fold away: the subtree's
  1101	/// unioned capability badges, one pin glyph carrying the subtree's total
  1102	/// unresolved-marker count, and — via `collect_findings_subtree` — the
  1103	/// subtree's finding count. No fn chips, no nested boxes: everything below
  1104	/// this box is folded into those counts.
  1105	fn render_collapsed_component(
  1106	    name: &str,
  1107	    qualified: &str,
  1108	    comp: &Component,
  1109	    profiles: &IndexMap<String, Vec<String>>,
  1110	    ctx: &FindingCtx,
  1111	) -> ComponentBox {
  1112	    let findings = collect_findings_subtree(qualified, comp, ctx);
  1113	    let ceiling = component_ceiling(comp);
  1114	    let (n_components, n_fns) = count_subtree(comp);
  1115	    let contents_line = format!(
  1116	        "{n_components} component{} \u{b7} {n_fns} fn{}",
  1117	        if n_components == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
  1118	        if n_fns == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
  1119	    );
  1120	    let badges = union_badges_subtree(comp);
  1121	    let unresolved = collect_unresolved_subtree(comp);
  1122	
  1123	    let finding_badge_w = finding_badge_width(&findings);
  1124	    let name_w = text_w(name, NAME_CHAR_W)
  1125	        + if findings.is_empty() {
  1126	            0.0
  1127	        } else {
  1128	            BADGE_GAP + finding_badge_w
  1129	        };
  1130	    let anchor_w = text_w(&comp.anchor, SUB_CHAR_W);
  1131	    let contents_w = text_w(&contents_line, SUB_CHAR_W);
  1132	    // Three header lines: name, anchor, contents — one more than the
  1133	    // baseline two-line header (`render_component`'s `owns_line` grows its
  1134	    // own header the same way, for the same reason).
  1135	    let header_h = HEADER_H + LINE_H;
  1136	
  1137	    let badges_row_w: f64 = badges
  1138	        .iter()
  1139	        .map(|b| text_w(b, BADGE_CHAR_W) + BADGE_PAD * 2.0 + BADGE_GAP)
  1140	        .sum();
  1141	    let pin_label = unresolved.len().to_string();
  1142	    let pin_w = if unresolved.is_empty() {
  1143	        0.0
  1144	    } else {
  1145	        PIN_R * 2.0 + text_w(&pin_label, CHIP_CHAR_W) + BADGE_GAP
  1146	    };
  1147	    let footer_row_w = badges_row_w + pin_w;
  1148	    let footer_row_h = if footer_row_w > 0.0 {
  1149	        BADGE_H.max(PIN_R * 2.0) + GAP
  1150	    } else {
  1151	        0.0
  1152	    };
  1153	
  1154	    let content_w = [
  1155	        name_w,
  1156	        anchor_w,
  1157	        contents_w,
  1158	        footer_row_w,
  1159	        MIN_BOX_W - PAD * 2.0,
  1160	    ]
  1161	    .into_iter()
  1162	    .fold(0.0_f64, f64::max);
  1163	    let box_w = content_w + PAD * 2.0;
  1164	    let box_h = PAD + header_h + footer_row_h + PAD;
  1165	
  1166	    let mut body = String::new();
  1167	    let y = PAD + header_h;
  1168	    if footer_row_w > 0.0 {
  1169	        let mut bx = PAD;
  1170	        for b in &badges {
  1171	            let bw = text_w(b, BADGE_CHAR_W) + BADGE_PAD * 2.0;
  1172	            body.push_str(&format!(
  1173	                "<g class=\"cap-badge\">{tip}<rect x=\"{bx:.1}\" y=\"{y:.1}\" width=\"{bw:.1}\" height=\"{BADGE_H:.1}\" rx=\"3\" /><text x=\"{tx:.1}\" y=\"{ty:.1}\">{label}</text></g>",
  1174	                tx = bx + BADGE_PAD,
  1175	                ty = y + BADGE_H - 6.0,
  1176	                label = esc(b),
  1177	                tip = title(&format!(
  1178	                    "this component may use `{b}` somewhere in its folded subtree. A \
  1179	                     component may use only the capabilities it declares — using an \
  1180	                     undeclared one is an architecture finding (§5.3, A0404)."
  1181	                ))
  1182	            ));
  1183	            bx += bw + BADGE_GAP;
  1184	        }
  1185	        if !unresolved.is_empty() {
  1186	            let cx = bx + PIN_R;
  1187	            let cy = y + BADGE_H / 2.0;
  1188	            let mut pin_tip = vec![format!(
  1189	                "{} unresolved decision{} folded inside this collapsed component:",
  1190	                unresolved.len(),
  1191	                if unresolved.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }
  1192	            )];
  1193	            for (id, note) in &unresolved {
  1194	                pin_tip.push(format!("#{id} — {note}"));
  1195	            }
  1196	            body.push_str(&format!(
  1197	                "<g class=\"unresolved-pin\">{tip}<circle cx=\"{cx:.1}\" cy=\"{cy:.1}\" r=\"{PIN_R:.1}\" /><text class=\"pin-label\" x=\"{cx:.1}\" y=\"{ty:.1}\">{label}</text></g>",
  1198	                ty = cy + 4.0,
  1199	                label = esc(&pin_label),
  1200	                tip = title(&pin_tip.join("\n"))
  1201	            ));
  1202	        }
  1203	    }
  1204	
  1205	    let mut tip = component_tip_lines(name, comp, profiles, &findings);
  1206	    tip.push(format!(
  1207	        "collapsed — {n_components} component{} and {n_fns} function{} folded inside; render \
  1208	         with --depth or --focus <name> to expand",
  1209	        if n_components == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
  1210	        if n_fns == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
  1730	    // ---- qualified-name resolution, position-independent --------------
  1731	    // `resolve()` only needs a positions map's *keys* (for the dotted-path
  1732	    // branch) and a leaf index built from those same keys (for the bare-name
  1733	    // branch) to settle §5.1a rule 6 ambiguity; the Rect values themselves
  1734	    // are unused until Phase 2 below, so a placeholder positions map lets us
  1735	    // resolve edges once, early, for ranking — and again later against real
  1736	    // coordinates, guaranteed to agree since both are the same lookup over
  1737	    // the same keys.
  1738	    let mut prelim_positions: IndexMap<String, Rect> = IndexMap::new();
  1739	    for (name, cbox) in &top {
  1740	        prelim_positions.entry(name.clone()).or_insert(Rect {
  1741	            x: 0.0,
  1742	            y: 0.0,
  1743	            w: 0.0,
  1744	            h: 0.0,
  1745	        });
  1746	        for (n, _) in &cbox.positions {
  1747	            prelim_positions
  1748	                .entry(format!("{name}.{n}"))
  1749	                .or_insert(Rect {
  1750	                    x: 0.0,
  1751	                    y: 0.0,
  1752	                    w: 0.0,
  1753	                    h: 0.0,
  1754	                });
  1755	        }
  1756	    }
  1757	    let prelim_leaf_index = leaf_index_of(&prelim_positions);
  1758	
  1759	    // ---- layered top-level layout (finding 1: stub call edges) ---------
  1760	    // Every edge endpoint maps to the top-level ancestor of the component it
  1761	    // resolves to — a nested component moves with its parent box, so only
  1762	    // top-level placement needs ranking (see layout.rs).
  1763	    let top_names: Vec<String> = top.iter().map(|(n, _)| n.clone()).collect();
  1764	    let mut rank_edges: Vec<(String, String)> = Vec::new();
  1765	    for e in &doc.edges {
  1766	        let Ok(edge) = parse_edge(e) else { continue };
  1767	        let Some((from_q, _)) = resolve(&edge.from, &prelim_positions, &prelim_leaf_index)? else {
  1768	            continue;
  1769	        };
  1770	        let Some((to_q, _)) = resolve(&edge.to, &prelim_positions, &prelim_leaf_index)? else {
  1771	            continue;
  1772	        };
  1773	        let top_of = |q: &str| q.split('.').next().unwrap_or(q).to_string();
  1774	        rank_edges.push((top_of(&from_q), top_of(&to_q)));
  1775	    }
  1776	    let sizes: IndexMap<String, (f64, f64)> = top
  1777	        .iter()
  1778	        .map(|(n, c)| (n.clone(), (c.width, c.height)))
  1779	        .collect();
  1780	    let layered = layout::layered_layout(&top_names, &rank_edges, &sizes, RANKSEP, NODESEP);
  1781	
  1782	    // ---- deny-driven margins (finding 3: clipped deny nodes) -----------
  1783	    // A `*` pseudo-node needs a reserved band clear of both the frame edge
  1784	    // and the nearest real box, wide enough for itself, a gap on each side,
  1785	    // and its own `except` label — sized from the label's actual text
  1786	    // rather than a guessed constant, so the reservation is always enough.
  1787	    // Kept alongside each deny's position in `doc.deny` (not the position in
  1788	    // this filtered vec) so a finding on an unparseable deny string — which
  1789	    // never makes it into this vec at all — still resolves to the right
  1790	    // `DenyIndex`, and so a parseable-but-flagged one attaches to the deny
  1791	    // it actually is rather than whichever survived the filter next to it.
  1792	    let parsed_denies: Vec<(usize, Deny)> = doc
  1793	        .deny
  1794	        .iter()
  1795	        .enumerate()
  1796	        .filter_map(|(i, d)| parse_deny(d).ok().map(|pd| (i, pd)))
  1797	        .collect();
  1798	    let mut extra_left = 0.0_f64;
  1799	    let mut extra_right = 0.0_f64;
  1800	    for (_, d) in &parsed_denies {
  1801	        // Reserved with the *worst-case* character width (`NAME_CHAR_W`,
  1802	        // the widest this renderer uses), not the narrower width the label
  1803	        // is actually drawn at (`SUB_CHAR_W`) — the same over-provisioning
  1804	        // `everything_renders_inside_the_canvas` already assumes when
  1805	        // checking the canvas boundary. Reserving exactly the drawn width
  1806	        // left zero clearance between the label and the first real box
  1807	        // (vetting 003 finding 4's "same clearance family").
  1808	        let label_w = if d.except.is_empty() {
  1809	            0.0
  1810	        } else {

  1847	                y: y + r.y,

  1868	                continue;

    93	/// A schema-valid document that failed to parse or validate. Carries the
    94	/// underlying serde-YAML message (which already names the offending field
    95	/// and, for `deny_unknown_fields`, suggests nothing further — The-Ply-Spec.md §5.1a
    96	/// leaves the nearest-known-key suggestion (E0204) to the full `ply check`
    97	/// implementation; this renderer only needs to refuse cleanly).
    98	#[derive(Debug)]
    99	pub struct ParseError(pub String);
   100	
   101	impl std::fmt::Display for ParseError {
   102	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   103	        write!(f, "{}", self.0)
   104	    }

   109	pub fn parse_document(yaml: &str) -> Result<Document, ParseError> {
