   111	}
   112	
   113	/// §5 item 1 / §5.1a rule 4: `test | fuzz(N)? | bounded(K)? | prove | mutate`.
   114	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   115	pub enum Check {
   116	    Test,
   117	    Fuzz(u32),
   118	    Bounded(u32),
   119	    Prove,
   120	    Mutate,
   121	}
   122	
   123	pub fn parse_check(s: &str) -> Result<Check, String> {
   124	    let s = s.trim();
   125	    match s {
   126	        "test" => return Ok(Check::Test),
   127	        "prove" => return Ok(Check::Prove),
   128	        "mutate" => return Ok(Check::Mutate),
   129	        _ => {}
   130	    }
   130	    }
   131	    if let Some(inner) = s.strip_prefix("fuzz(").and_then(|r| r.strip_suffix(')')) {
   132	        let n: u32 = inner
   133	            .trim()
   134	            .parse()
   135	            .map_err(|_| format!("invalid fuzz(N) count in {s:?}"))?;
   136	        if !(1..=1_000_000).contains(&n) {
   137	            return Err(format!(
   138	                "{s:?} is not a valid check: the number is how many random inputs get tried, \
   139	                 and it must be between 1 and 1,000,000"
   140	            ));
   141	        }
   142	        return Ok(Check::Fuzz(n));
   143	    }
   144	    if let Some(inner) = s.strip_prefix("bounded(").and_then(|r| r.strip_suffix(')')) {
   145	        let k: u32 = inner
   146	            .trim()
   147	            .parse()
   148	            .map_err(|_| format!("invalid bounded(K) count in {s:?}"))?;
   149	        if !(1..=64).contains(&k) {
   150	            return Err(format!(
   151	                "{s:?} is not a valid check: the number is how many times loops are unrolled \
   152	                 during the proof, and it must be between 1 and 64 — a bound of 0 would prove \
   153	                 nothing"
   154	            ));
   155	        }
   156	        return Ok(Check::Bounded(k));
   157	    }
   158	    Err(format!(
   159	        "{s:?} is not a check Ply knows: expected test, fuzz(N), bounded(K), prove, or mutate"
   160	    ))
   161	}
   162	
   163	/// §5 item 2: `A -> B` (call) or `A ~> B : path::Type` (data flow).
   164	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
   165	pub enum EdgeKind {
   166	    Call,
   167	    Flow(String),
   168	}
   169	
   170	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
   171	pub struct Edge {
   172	    pub from: String,
   173	    pub to: String,
   174	    pub kind: EdgeKind,
   175	}
   176	
   177	pub fn parse_edge(s: &str) -> Result<Edge, String> {
   178	    let s = s.trim();
   179	    if let Some(idx) = s.find("~>") {
   180	        let (left, right) = (s[..idx].trim(), s[idx + 2..].trim());
   181	        let mut parts = right.splitn(2, ':');
   182	        let to = parts.next().unwrap_or("").trim();
   183	        let ty = parts
   184	            .next()
   185	            .ok_or_else(|| {
   186	                format!(
   187	                    "{s:?} is missing its payload type: a data-flow edge is written \
   188	                     \"a ~> b : Type\""
   189	                )
   190	            })?
   191	            .trim();
   192	        if left.is_empty() || to.is_empty() {
   193	            return Err(format!(
   194	                "{s:?} is missing the component name on one side of the arrow"
   195	            ));
   196	        }
   197	        if ty.is_empty() {
   198	            return Err(format!(
   199	                "{s:?} is missing its payload type: a data-flow edge is written \"a ~> b : Type\""
   200	            ));
   201	        }
   202	        return Ok(Edge {
   203	            from: left.to_string(),
   204	            to: to.to_string(),
   205	            kind: EdgeKind::Flow(ty.to_string()),
   206	        });
   207	    }
   208	    if let Some(idx) = s.find("->") {
   209	        let (left, right) = (s[..idx].trim(), s[idx + 2..].trim());
   210	        if left.is_empty() || right.is_empty() {
   211	            return Err(format!(
   212	                "{s:?} is missing the component name on one side of the arrow"
   213	            ));
   214	        }
   215	        return Ok(Edge {
   216	            from: left.to_string(),
   217	            to: right.to_string(),
   218	            kind: EdgeKind::Call,
   219	        });
   220	    }
   221	    Err(format!(
   222	        "{s:?} is not an edge: expected \"a -> b\" (a may call b) or \"a ~> b : Type\" \
   223	         (data flows from a to b)"
   224	    ))
   225	}
   226	
   227	/// §5 item 3: `PAT -> PAT [except C1, C2]` where `PAT := IDENT | *`.
   228	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
   229	pub struct Deny {
   230	    pub from: String,
   231	    pub to: String,
   232	    pub except: Vec<String>,
   233	}
   234	
   235	pub fn parse_deny(s: &str) -> Result<Deny, String> {
   236	    let s = s.trim();
   237	    let (main, except_part) = match s.find("except") {
   238	        Some(idx) => (s[..idx].trim(), s[idx + "except".len()..].trim()),
   239	        None => (s, ""),
   240	    };
   241	    let idx = main.find("->").ok_or_else(|| {
   242	        format!("{s:?} is not a deny rule: expected \"a -> b\" or \"a -> b except c, d\"")
   243	    })?;
   244	    let from = main[..idx].trim();
   245	    let to = main[idx + 2..].trim();
     1	//! Document-local `ply.yaml` validation (The-Ply-Spec.md §5.1a, §5.1, §5.6) — the
     2	//! subset of `cargo ply check` (§6) that needs no anchored Rust code:
     3	//! unknown fields, schema shape, and required fields are already rejected
     4	//! by `ply_model::parse_document`'s strict serde parse (nothing to
     5	//! duplicate here). This crate only flags constructs that parse cleanly but
     6	//! still violate The-Ply-Spec.md. Anchor resolution, staleness, and the
     7	//! architecture rules (§5.2, §5.3) need real code behind the anchors and
     8	//! are out of scope.
     9	
    10	use ply_model::{Check, Component, Document, Edge, parse_check, parse_deny, parse_edge};
    11	use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
    12	
    13	/// Where a diagnostic attaches for drawing (The-Ply-Spec.md §7.1 "finding" row).
    14	/// `ply-render` consumes this to know what to mark red; `ply-check`'s own
    15	/// stdout/exit-code contract never prints it (see `Diagnostic`'s `Display`).
    16	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    17	pub enum Target {
    18	    /// A fn claim, named by its component's fully qualified dotted path
    19	    /// (top-level component name, or `parent.child` for nested ones) plus
    20	    /// its own fn key.
    21	    Fn {
    22	        component_path: String,
    23	        fn_name: String,
    24	    },
    25	    /// A component (its box), named by the same qualified path.
    26	    Component(String),
    27	    /// An entry in `doc.edges`, by position.
    28	    EdgeIndex(usize),
    29	    /// An entry in `doc.deny`, by position.
    30	    DenyIndex(usize),
    31	    /// An unresolved marker, by its declared id — may attach to more than
    32	    /// one drawn pin if that id is a duplicate (that's the finding).
    33	    UnresolvedId(u64),
    34	    /// No single item in the document is the offender; the renderer falls
    35	    /// back to a count next to the workspace title.
    36	    Document,
    37	}
    38	
    39	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    40	pub struct Diagnostic {
    41	    pub code: &'static str,
    42	    pub message: String,
    43	    pub target: Target,
    44	}
    45	
    46	impl std::fmt::Display for Diagnostic {
    47	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    48	        write!(f, "{}: {}", self.code, self.message)
    49	    }
    50	}
    51	
    52	impl Diagnostic {
    53	    /// Severity, derived from the code prefix (The-Ply-Spec.md §5.3: item-tier
    54	    /// rules are `W`-severity by default). A `W`-code is advisory — `ply
    55	    /// check` reports it, but it must not by itself fail the run; every other
    56	    /// code this crate emits is a document-local error.
    57	    pub fn is_advisory(&self) -> bool {
    58	        self.code.starts_with('W')
    59	    }
    60	}
    61	
    62	fn diag(code: &'static str, message: String, target: Target) -> Diagnostic {
    63	    Diagnostic {
    64	        code,
    65	        message,
    66	        target,
    67	    }
    68	}
    69	
    70	/// §5.1a rule 3: anchors and fn keys are plain segment paths —
    71	/// `IDENT(::IDENT)*`, where a segment may also be a type name in
    72	/// `Type::method` position. No generics, no trait-qualified paths, no
    73	/// lifetimes — anything else is `E0304 unsupported path form`.
    74	fn is_valid_path_form(s: &str) -> bool {
    75	    !s.is_empty()
    76	        && s.split("::").all(|seg| {
    77	            let mut chars = seg.chars();
    78	            matches!(chars.next(), Some(c) if c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_')
    79	                && chars.all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_')
    80	        })
    81	}
    82	
    83	/// §5.1 / §5.1a rule 4: every check string must parse under the real
    84	/// `test | fuzz(N)? | bounded(K)? | prove | mutate` micro-syntax, numeric
    85	/// bounds included. A string serde accepted as a plain `String` but that
    86	/// fails this parser is `E0203`.
    87	fn check_syntax(checks: &[String], location: &str, target: &Target, out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    88	    for c in checks {
    89	        if let Err(e) = parse_check(c) {
    90	            out.push(diag("E0203", format!("{e} ({location})"), target.clone()));
    91	        }
    92	    }
    93	}
    94	
    95	/// §5.1: `mutate` without a `test` or `fuzz` entry in the *same* checks list
    96	/// is `E0504`. Checked independently wherever a checks list appears
    97	/// (a component's default checks, or a fn claim's own) — this rule does
    98	/// not merge inherited and explicit checks, since that merge isn't spelled
    99	/// out anywhere as part of the document-local grammar.
   100	fn check_mutate_rule(
   101	    checks: &[String],
   102	    location: &str,
   103	    target: &Target,
   104	    out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>,
   105	) {
   250	/// taken as the fully qualified path it already looks like.
   251	fn resolve_component_ref(
   252	    token: &str,
   253	    leaf_index: &HashMap<String, Vec<String>>,
   254	    all_qualified: &HashSet<String>,
   255	) -> Option<String> {
   256	    if token == "*" {
   257	        return None;
   258	    }
   259	    if token.contains('.') {
   260	        return all_qualified.contains(token).then(|| token.to_string());
   261	    }
   262	    match leaf_index.get(token) {
   263	        Some(paths) if paths.len() == 1 => Some(paths[0].clone()),
   264	        _ => None,
   265	    }
   266	}
   267	
   268	/// True if `other` is `ancestor` itself plus at least one more dotted
   269	/// segment — i.e. `ancestor` is a strict prefix of `other` ending on a `.`
   270	/// boundary, matching how nested qualified paths are built in
   271	/// `walk_component`.
   272	fn is_strict_ancestor(ancestor: &str, other: &str) -> bool {
   273	    other.len() > ancestor.len()
   274	        && other.starts_with(ancestor)
   275	        && other.as_bytes()[ancestor.len()] == b'.'
   276	}
   277	
   278	/// §5.3: an edge whose two endpoints lie on one nesting line — a component
   279	/// and its own descendant, either direction, at any depth — is redundant:
   280	/// containment already grants the permission the edge would declare.
   281	/// Applies to both edge kinds (`->` and `~>`); the spec paragraph states the
   282	/// rule in terms of "an explicit edge" and closes with "Edges are for
   283	/// crossings between nesting lines", neither qualified to calls only.
   284	fn check_containment_redundancy(
   285	    edge_str: &str,
   286	    edge: &Edge,
   287	    leaf_index: &HashMap<String, Vec<String>>,
   288	    all_qualified: &HashSet<String>,
   289	    target: &Target,
   290	    out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>,
   291	) {
   292	    let Some(from) = resolve_component_ref(&edge.from, leaf_index, all_qualified) else {
   293	        return;
   294	    };
   295	    let Some(to) = resolve_component_ref(&edge.to, leaf_index, all_qualified) else {
   296	        return;
   297	    };
   298	
   299	    let (outer, inner) = if is_strict_ancestor(&from, &to) {
   300	        (from, to)
   301	    } else if is_strict_ancestor(&to, &from) {
   302	        (to, from)
   303	    } else {
   304	        return;
   305	    };
   306	
   307	    out.push(diag(
   308	        "W0409",
   309	        format!(
   310	            "\"edge {}\" is redundant: {inner} is inside {outer}, and a component may always \
   311	             call within its own nesting line — no edge needed",
   312	            edge_str.trim(),
   313	        ),
   314	        target.clone(),
   315	    ));
   316	}
   317	
   318	pub fn run_checks(doc: &Document) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
   319	    let mut out = Vec::new();
   320	    let mut unresolved_ids: Vec<(u64, String)> = Vec::new();
   321	    let mut leaf_index: HashMap<String, Vec<String>> = HashMap::new();
   322	
   323	    for (name, c) in &doc.components {
   324	        walk_component(
   325	            name,
   326	            name,
   327	            c,
   328	            &mut out,
   329	            &mut unresolved_ids,
   330	            &mut leaf_index,
   331	        );
   332	    }
   333	
   334	    // Every qualified path any component actually has, used to resolve a
   335	    // dotted edge endpoint literally (§5.1a rule 6's dotted form) without
   336	    // re-deriving it from layout.
   337	    let all_qualified: HashSet<String> = leaf_index.values().flatten().cloned().collect();
   338	
   339	    for (i, e) in doc.edges.iter().enumerate() {
   340	        let target = Target::EdgeIndex(i);
   341	        match parse_edge(e) {
   342	            Ok(edge) => {
   343	                check_token_ambiguity(&edge.from, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   344	                check_token_ambiguity(&edge.to, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   345	                check_containment_redundancy(
   346	                    e,
   347	                    &edge,
   348	                    &leaf_index,
   349	                    &all_qualified,
   350	                    &target,
   351	                    &mut out,
   352	                );
   353	            }
   354	            Err(err) => out.push(diag("E0203", format!("{err} (edges)"), target)),
   355	        }
   356	    }
   357	    for (i, d) in doc.deny.iter().enumerate() {
   358	        let target = Target::DenyIndex(i);
   359	        match parse_deny(d) {
   360	            Ok(deny) => {
   361	                check_token_ambiguity(&deny.from, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   362	                check_token_ambiguity(&deny.to, &leaf_index, &target, &mut out);
   363	            }
   364	            Err(err) => out.push(diag("E0203", format!("{err} (deny)"), target)),
   365	        }
   366	    }
   367	
   368	    for entry in &doc.unresolved {
   369	        unresolved_ids.push((entry.id, "registry".to_string()));
   370	    }
   371	    // §5.1a rule 5: unresolved ids are unique across the whole merged
   372	    // workspace, registry and fn entries together. Report in encounter
   373	    // order (component walk, then top-level registry) so output is
   374	    // deterministic without needing a stable-sorted map.
   375	    let mut first_seen: HashMap<u64, String> = HashMap::new();
   376	    for (id, location) in &unresolved_ids {
   377	        match first_seen.get(id) {
   378	            Some(prev) => out.push(diag(
   379	                "E0205",
   380	                format!(
   381	                    "unresolved id {id} is used twice ({prev} and {location}): each open \
   382	                     decision needs its own number"
   383	                ),
   384	                Target::UnresolvedId(*id),
   385	            )),
   386	            None => {
   387	                first_seen.insert(*id, location.clone());
   388	            }
   389	        }
   390	    }
    75	}
    76	
    77	/// The-Ply-Spec.md §0 / §7: "Statuses ... do not sit in that [evidence] order; they
    78	/// propagate upward as flags and open-item counts alongside it." This is the
    79	/// status vocabulary named in §0's glossary row for `status`, minus
    80	/// `conditional` -- see [`VerdictNode::conditional`] for why that one moved
    81	/// out of this set.
    82	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
    83	pub enum StatusKind {
    84	    Stale,
    85	    WeakSpec,
    86	    Unsupported,
    87	    EngineMissing,
    88	    Timeout,
    89	    Inconclusive,
    90	}
    91	
    92	/// One node of a verdict tree (The-Ply-Spec.md §7), reduced to exactly what
    93	/// [`aggregate`] needs: this node's own claim ([`NodeKind`]), its own status
    94	/// flags, its own conditional assumptions (if any), and its children.
    95	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    96	pub struct VerdictNode {
    97	    pub kind: NodeKind,
    98	    pub statuses: BTreeSet<StatusKind>,
    99	    /// The-Ply-Spec.md §7 / D5: "`conditional` structurally carries its assumptions:
   100	    /// a conditional status without an assumptions list is unrepresentable
   101	    /// in the kernel, not validated against." `None` = not conditional;
   102	    /// `Some(assumptions)` = conditional, with exactly the assumptions it
   103	    /// rests on riding along in the same value -- there is no way to
   104	    /// construct "conditional" without also supplying the list, because
   105	    /// they are the same field rather than a flag plus an independent one
   106	    /// that could disagree with it.
   107	    pub conditional: Option<Vec<String>>,
   108	    pub children: Vec<VerdictNode>,
   109	}
   110	
   111	/// The result of [`aggregate`] at one tree position: this node's subtree
   112	/// (itself plus every descendant) folded into `worst_descendant`, unioned
   113	/// `statuses`, unioned conditional assumptions, and a total `open_items`
   114	/// count. Mirrors the input tree's shape via `children`.
   115	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
   116	pub struct AggregatedNode {
   117	    /// The-Ply-Spec.md §7's `worst_descendant`: the worst-of (D6) over every
   118	    /// *claimable* node's own evidence in the subtree. `Evidence::Unclaimed`
   119	    /// exactly when the subtree contains no claimable node at all (§7: "A
   120	    /// container with no claimable descendants reads `unclaimed`").
   121	    pub evidence: Evidence,
   122	    /// Union of this node's own `statuses` and every descendant's own
   123	    /// `statuses` -- D6/§7: "statuses ... propagate upward as flags."
   124	    pub statuses: BTreeSet<StatusKind>,
   125	    /// Sorted, deduplicated union of the assumptions carried by every node
   126	    /// in the subtree (self included) whose own [`VerdictNode::conditional`]
   127	    /// is `Some(_)`. `None` exactly when no node in the subtree is
   128	    /// conditional -- this is standing obligation 2: a conditional status
   129	    /// never reaches an ancestor without the assumptions that justify it.
   130	    pub conditional: Option<Vec<String>>,
   131	    /// The-Ply-Spec.md §7's `open_items`, folded as a count: "`open_items` counts
   132	    /// flag instances, not flagged nodes: a node carrying two statuses
   133	    /// contributes 2." This kernel counts every `StatusKind` flag plus a
   134	    /// `conditional` (when present) as one flag instance each, summed over
   135	    /// every node in the subtree. Unresolved markers (`ply.yaml` registry
   136	    /// entries, §5.6) have no representation in this kernel's node type --
   137	    /// they anchor to code the kernel never sees -- so they are not part of
   138	    /// this count; a model layer that also tracks them would add its own
   139	    /// count to this one.
   140	    pub open_items: usize,
   141	    pub children: Vec<AggregatedNode>,
   142	}
   143	
   144	fn merge_conditional(a: Option<Vec<String>>, b: Option<Vec<String>>) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
   145	    match (a, b) {
   146	        (None, None) => None,
   147	        (Some(v), None) | (None, Some(v)) => Some(v),
   148	        (Some(mut v), Some(w)) => {
   149	            v.extend(w);
   150	            Some(v)
   151	        }
   152	    }
   153	}
   154	
   155	/// Combines two "min over claimable-only evidence so far" accumulators,
   156	/// where `None` means "no claimable node found yet" rather than any real
   157	/// evidence value. Not-yet-found must be a true no-op here, never folded in
   158	/// through `Evidence::min` as if `Unclaimed` were a placeholder identity:
   159	/// `Unclaimed` is a real, comparable rung (weaker than `Tested` but
   160	/// stronger than `Violation`), so treating "nothing here" as literal
   161	/// `Unclaimed` and folding it via `.min()` would let an empty container
   162	/// wrongly drag down a genuinely stronger claimable sibling or child. (An
   163	/// earlier version of `aggregate` did exactly this -- see the note on
   164	/// `aggregate_raw` below.)
   165	fn combine_claimable(a: Option<Evidence>, b: Option<Evidence>) -> Option<Evidence> {
   166	    match (a, b) {
   167	        (None, x) => x,
   168	        (x, None) => x,
   169	        (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)),
   170	    }
   171	}
   172	
   173	/// The recursive core of [`aggregate`]. Returns the subtree's "min over
   174	/// claimable-only evidence" as an `Option<Evidence>` (`None` = no claimable
   175	/// node anywhere in the subtree) *alongside* the public [`AggregatedNode`]
   176	/// for this position, whose own `evidence` field is that option already
   177	/// defaulted to `Evidence::Unclaimed` for display (The-Ply-Spec.md §7: "A container
   178	/// with no claimable descendants reads `unclaimed`").
   179	///
   180	/// The two-value return exists because those are genuinely different
   180	/// The two-value return exists because those are genuinely different
   181	/// things: a parent folding its children must be able to tell "this child
   182	/// subtree has no claimable node at all" (skip it -- it contributes
   183	/// nothing) apart from "this child subtree's real answer happens to be the
   184	/// value `Unclaimed`" (a genuine claimable leaf that earned no checks --
   185	/// fold it in like any other evidence value). Both display as `Unclaimed`
   186	/// on that child's own `AggregatedNode`, but only the raw `Option` lets an
   187	/// ancestor two levels up combine them correctly. Collapsing to the
   188	/// defaulted `Evidence` one level too early -- i.e. having `aggregate`
   189	/// call itself recursively and fold children's already-defaulted
   190	/// `agg.evidence` via a plain `.min()` -- is precisely the bug
   191	/// `tests/enumeration.rs` caught: a bare container with one
   192	/// `Claimable(Tested)` child aggregated to `Unclaimed` instead of `Tested`,
   193	/// because the container's own placeholder `Unclaimed` won the `.min()`
   194	/// against the real, stronger child.
   195	fn aggregate_raw(node: &VerdictNode) -> (Option<Evidence>, AggregatedNode) {
   196	    let mut raw_evidence = match node.kind {
   197	        NodeKind::Claimable(e) => Some(e),
   198	        NodeKind::Container => None,
   199	    };
   200	    let mut statuses: BTreeSet<StatusKind> = node.statuses.clone();
   201	    let mut conditional = node.conditional.clone();
   202	    let mut open_items = node.statuses.len() + if node.conditional.is_some() { 1 } else { 0 };
   203	    let mut children = Vec::with_capacity(node.children.len());
   204	
   205	    for child in &node.children {
   206	        let (child_raw, child_agg) = aggregate_raw(child);
   207	        raw_evidence = combine_claimable(raw_evidence, child_raw);
   208	        statuses.extend(child_agg.statuses.iter().copied());
   209	        conditional = merge_conditional(conditional, child_agg.conditional.clone());
   210	        open_items += child_agg.open_items;
   211	        children.push(child_agg);
   212	    }
   213	
   214	    if let Some(c) = &mut conditional {
   215	        c.sort();
   216	        c.dedup();
   217	    }
   218	
   219	    let evidence = raw_evidence.unwrap_or(Evidence::Unclaimed);
   220	    (
   221	        raw_evidence,
   222	        AggregatedNode {
   223	            evidence,
   224	            statuses,
   225	            conditional,
   226	            open_items,
   227	            children,
   228	        },
   229	    )
   230	}
   231	
   232	/// Fold a verdict tree into its per-node aggregated results (The-Ply-Spec.md §7,
   233	/// D6). Pure: no I/O, no randomness, no shared mutable state -- calling it
   234	/// twice on equal inputs always yields equal outputs (standing obligation
   235	/// 4). Every collection used for aggregated state (`BTreeSet`, plus a
   236	/// sorted+deduplicated `Vec` for conditional assumptions) is
   237	/// order-independent by construction, specifically to avoid the classic
   238	/// footgun where a hash-based set's iteration order can differ between two
   239	/// otherwise-equal instances (Rust's `HashSet`/`HashMap` seed their hasher
   240	/// per instance) -- that would make two `AggregatedNode` values compare
   241	/// unequal, or print differently, for no reason tied to the data itself.
   242	pub fn aggregate(node: &VerdictNode) -> AggregatedNode {
   243	    aggregate_raw(node).1
   244	}
   245	
   246	#[cfg(test)]
   247	mod tests {
   248	    use super::*;
   249	
   250	    fn leaf(kind: NodeKind) -> VerdictNode {
   251	        VerdictNode {
   252	            kind,
   253	            statuses: BTreeSet::new(),
   254	            conditional: None,
   255	            children: Vec::new(),
   256	        }
   257	    }
   258	
   259	    #[test]
   260	    fn evidence_order_matches_d6() {
   261	        assert!(Evidence::Violation < Evidence::Unclaimed);
   262	        assert!(Evidence::Unclaimed < Evidence::Tested);
   263	        assert!(Evidence::Tested < Evidence::Fuzzed);
   264	        assert!(Evidence::Fuzzed < Evidence::Bounded);
   265	        assert!(Evidence::Bounded < Evidence::Proved);
   266	    }
   267	
   268	    #[test]
   269	    fn a_single_claimable_leaf_aggregates_to_its_own_evidence() {
   270	        let agg = aggregate(&leaf(NodeKind::Claimable(Evidence::Proved)));
   271	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, Evidence::Proved);
   272	        assert!(agg.statuses.is_empty());
   273	        assert!(agg.conditional.is_none());
   274	        assert_eq!(agg.open_items, 0);
   275	    }
   276	
   277	    #[test]
   278	    fn an_empty_container_aggregates_to_unclaimed() {
   279	        let agg = aggregate(&leaf(NodeKind::Container));
   280	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, Evidence::Unclaimed);
   400	    impl SymTree {
   401	        fn build(self) -> VerdictNode {
   402	            let mut children = Vec::new();
   403	            if self.has_child_a {
   404	                children.push(self.child_a.into_node(Vec::new()));
   405	            }
   406	            if self.has_child_b {
   407	                children.push(self.child_b.into_node(Vec::new()));
   408	            }
   409	            self.root.into_node(children)
   410	        }
   411	
   412	        /// The min over claimable-only own-evidence across root + present
   413	        /// children, `None` if none of them is claimable -- computed
   414	        /// directly from the symbolic fields, not by calling `aggregate`.
   415	        fn expected_claimable_min(&self) -> Option<Evidence> {
   416	            let mut acc = self.root.own_evidence();
   417	            if self.has_child_a {
   418	                acc = match (acc, self.child_a.own_evidence()) {
   419	                    (None, x) => x,
   420	                    (x, None) => x,
   421	                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)),
   422	                };
   423	            }
   424	            if self.has_child_b {
   425	                acc = match (acc, self.child_b.own_evidence()) {
   426	                    (None, x) => x,
   427	                    (x, None) => x,
   428	                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)),
   429	                };
   430	            }
   431	            acc
   432	        }
   433	    }
   434	
   435	    /// Standing obligation 1 (as reworded by The-Ply-Spec.md §7's amendment):
   436	    /// worst-of never reports evidence stronger than the weakest claimable
   437	    /// node in the subtree, and reports exactly `Unclaimed` when there is
   438	    /// none. Standing obligation 3 (violation-reaches-root) is the case of
   439	    /// this where the weakest claimable node is a `Violation`.
   440	    #[kani::proof]
   441	    fn proof_worst_of_evidence() {
   442	        let t: SymTree = kani::any();
   443	        let expected = t.expected_claimable_min().unwrap_or(Evidence::Unclaimed);
   444	
   445	        let agg = aggregate(&t.build());
   446	        assert_eq!(agg.evidence, expected);
   447	
   448	        let any_violation = t.root.own_evidence() == Some(Evidence::Violation)
   449	            || (t.has_child_a && t.child_a.own_evidence() == Some(Evidence::Violation))
   450	            || (t.has_child_b && t.child_b.own_evidence() == Some(Evidence::Violation));
   451	        if any_violation {
   452	            assert_eq!(agg.evidence, Evidence::Violation);
   453	        }
   454	    }
   455	
   456	    /// Standing obligation 2: `conditional` never disappears without its
   457	    /// assumptions.
   458	    #[kani::proof]
   459	    fn proof_conditional_carries_its_assumptions() {
   460	        let t: SymTree = kani::any();
   461	        let any_conditional = t.root.conditional
   462	            || (t.has_child_a && t.child_a.conditional)
   463	            || (t.has_child_b && t.child_b.conditional);
   464	
   465	        let agg = aggregate(&t.build());
   466	        assert_eq!(agg.conditional.is_some(), any_conditional);
   467	        if any_conditional {
   468	            assert!(!agg.conditional.unwrap().is_empty());
   469	        }
   470	    }
   471	
   472	    /// Standing obligation 4: aggregating the same tree twice yields
   473	    /// identical results.
   474	    #[kani::proof]
   475	    fn proof_aggregate_is_deterministic() {
   476	        let t: SymTree = kani::any();
   477	        let a = aggregate(&t.build());
   478	        let b = aggregate(&t.build());
   479	        assert_eq!(a, b);
   480	    }
   481

codex
The current suite is green, including the 991,389-tree enumeration and 44 renderer integration tests. That is credible evidence for the pure aggregation function and for a small static SVG fixture set; it is not evidence that `cargo ply` can route, execute, or authenticate a single real engine result. I’ve finished the repo pass and am consolidating the assessment around that distinction.
codex
## Bottom line

Ply has a sound product boundary, but the project has validated the least risky 20% of it. Today it is a strong specification prototype, a useful static renderer, and a carefully tested aggregation kernel—not yet evidence that the proposed verification layer will work.

The core danger is semantic impedance at the engine boundary. “Glue” becomes a compiler: attributes, contracts, harnesses, generators, stubs, replay artifacts, engine parsers, fingerprints, and provenance all have to preserve meaning. That is much harder than subprocess orchestration.

## 1. Is “route to existing engines” sound?

Yes as a constraint. Building an SMT solver, model checker, or proof engine would destroy the project’s scope. The spec is right in §1: “We build glue and UX, never solvers.”

Where it breaks:

- Harness construction is the real product. Vetting 001 shows that `Spsc::try_push` and `try_pop` are not meaningfully fuzzable because the receiver is private, invariant-heavy state. A constructor hook only creates empty queues; it cannot generate reachable full states or call sequences. This is a fundamental gap, not a missing adapter.
- Contract semantics differ sharply between Kani, proptest, cargo-mutants, and Verus. `requires` as Kani assumptions, proptest rejection filters, and test preconditions are not interchangeable.
- Rust syntax and behavior exceed the contract subset quickly. Vetting 002 already found missing arrays and `matches!` bindings.
- `syn` extraction cannot make architecture enforcement sound. D4 admits this, but `strict: true` does not fix false negatives from macros, trait dispatch, aliases, generated code, or unresolved calls.
- D5’s modular composition becomes conditional for common cases: cross-crate calls, fuzzed callees, cycles, and generic instantiation mismatches. That may make the result technically honest but practically unattractive.
- Counterexample normalization is high-risk. D7 requires replayable Kani data plus stable Rust tests. The adapter must prove that the witness actually reproduces; currently no such pipeline exists.

The bet is viable only if M0 proves one narrow vertical slice with real Kani behavior. Until then, it is a strategy, not an established fact.

## 2. Was the verdict kernel the right first thing?

It was the right first pure artifact, but the wrong first product-risk test.

The kernel has genuinely good properties:

- `Evidence` makes the ordering explicit: `Violation < Unclaimed < Tested < Fuzzed < Bounded < Proved`.
- `Option<Evidence>` correctly distinguishes “no claimable descendants” from a real `Unclaimed` claim (`tools/kernel/src/lib.rs:155-170`).
- The exhaustive test checks 991,389 finite trees against an independent oracle. The fault-injection demo shows it catches both best-of aggregation and dropped assumptions.
- The implementation is small, pure, dependency-free, and easy to reason about.

But its scope is narrower than “Ply’s verdict semantics”:

- The enumeration covers trees of at most four nodes, 21 reduced node configurations, one representative status, and one fixed conditional assumption string (`tools/kernel/tests/enumeration.rs`).
- Kani harnesses are present but have not been run; `TODO.md:27-28` explicitly says to run them once Kani is installed.
- `unresolved` items are not represented in the kernel at all. The kernel comment says the model layer must add them later (`tools/kernel/src/lib.rs:131-139`), so the actual `open_items` contract remains unproved.
- Conditional assumptions are opaque strings, not linked to node IDs, callee hashes, verdict provenance, or actual call edges.
- `Some(vec![])` is representable, despite the comment claiming a conditional without assumptions is unrepresentable. The Kani harness never explores an empty assumption list because its symbolic shim always inserts one string.
- The “no rule sequence assigns one node two verdicts” obligation from `CLAUDE.md` is not modeled. The kernel returns one value by type construction; it does not test competing pipeline transitions, cached results, or merge behavior.
- The temporary `tmp_*` Kani harnesses are still in the production kernel (`tools/kernel/src/lib.rs:482+`).

So: good kernel, but it proves aggregation mechanics—not evidence authenticity, verdict lifecycle, or engine composition.

## 3. Is the visual grammar a good constraint?

Yes, mostly. It is not merely a gimmick.

It produced real design pressure:

- The black SVG failure exposed that structural tests were insufficient.
- `owns` was parsed but not rendered, violating the stated bijection.
- Vetting found unreadable glyphs, clipped deny rules, overlapping edges, and collisions inside nested boxes.
- The renderer now has useful invariants for style resolution, tooltips, canvas bounds, edge visibility, and overlap avoidance.

That justifies the constraint as a design gate for declarative architecture claims.

However, the current output does not justify the full product claim:

- It renders declared ceilings, not earned verdicts. The renderer explicitly says “none of it has been run yet” (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:749-764`).
- `strict` has no visual notch; it is only explained in a tooltip.
- `mode: synth` is parsed but has no visual treatment.
- `examples` are only mentioned in tooltips, not rendered as `e×N`.
- Statuses and actual verdicts are absent.
- Inline attributes are invisible because the renderer consumes YAML directly.
- `~>` flows are drawn while explicitly unverified in v1.

This directly contradicts the spec’s “Gate debt: none” claim. `TODO.md:25-26` still lists visual debt for `strict`, `mode: synth`, and `examples`.

The visual grammar is valuable for the architecture view. It should not become the project’s main development surface before an actual engine-produced counterexample exists.

## 4. Where is the spec self-deceiving or vague?

### §5 and D3: the “normative schema” does not exist

The spec says `schema/ply.schema.json` is normative, embedded, versioned, and golden-tested (§5, D3). The repository has no schema and no schema validator. `ply-model` only uses serde structs (`tools/model/src/lib.rs:12-110`).

Current gaps include:

- no `ply: 1` version enforcement;
- no identifier validation for component/profile names;
- no line/column mapping;
- no multi-file merge;
- no duplicate component detection across files;
- no nearest-key suggestions;
- no profile-reference validation;
- no robust deny/edge lexical validation.

The current `parse_deny` accepts surprisingly loose input (`tools/model/src/lib.rs:235-258`). That is not yet a normative grammar.

### §5.1: component default checks are underspecified and currently ignored

The spec says component-level `checks` are “default checks for all fns in scope” (§5.1, line 225). But `tools/check/src/lib.rs:95-99` explicitly does not merge inherited and function checks, and the renderer’s ceiling computation only reads `fc.checks` (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:434-448`).

This means declared ceilings and eventual verification behavior can disagree before any engine is involved.

### §5.2 and D14: fingerprints are underspecified

“Token-stream hash, merged contract text, callee contract hashes, engine version, flags, features, target” is not enough to define reproducibility.

Unanswered questions:

- Which rustc version?
- Which dependency lockfile?
- Which macro expansion?
- Which environment variables?
- Which generated harness?
- Which `trusted` evidence?
- Which generator implementation?
- How are equivalent contract expressions normalized?
- What exactly does `accept` attest to?

Without a precise lock format and provenance model, stale detection becomes ceremonial.

### §5.3: advisory extraction is honest, but not architecture enforcement

D4 correctly admits that `syn` is approximate. But a `strict` switch cannot turn incomplete extraction into a sound security boundary. The spec should say plainly:

> strict means “fail on detected violations,” not “prove no violation exists.”

The current tools have none of the extractor, coverage metric, anchor resolution, or architecture engine promised by §5.3 and D11.

### §5.4a–§5.4c: the contract system is still a collection of assumptions

Vetting 001 and 002 already found:

- stateful receiver generation is missing;
- arrays are omitted from §5.4b;
- `matches!` bindings are not defined;
- `old()` only covers a narrow two-state case;
- sequence properties and FIFO ordering remain inexpressible;
- test generation from contracts is not specified enough to know what inputs it can produce;
- high-rejection behavior for `requires` has no defined threshold or verdict effect.

The subset can be made useful, but it must be presented as a deliberately small fragment, not as a general contract language.

### §5.5 / D5: conditional evidence is structurally visible but semantically weak

The spec says the caller is conditional when it assumes a callee contract. It does not define:

- how the assumption is tied to a concrete callee claim;
- how generic instantiations are matched;
- how the caller’s precondition proves the callee’s `requires`;
- how stale or weak callee contracts affect the caller;
- how an assumption is discharged later.

Vetting 002 explicitly leaves generic reuse and instantiation mismatch unresolved.

### §7.1 and §8: the renderer contract is internally inconsistent

§7.1 says the renderer’s only input is the §8 envelope. The actual renderer takes `ply_model::Document`, runs `ply-check` itself, and computes declared ceilings directly (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:1666+`).

That is acceptable for a prototype renderer, but it does not validate the future envelope contract.

§8 itself is only an example JSON object, not a schema. The counterexample fields are mostly strings, and there is no formal guarantee that a reported witness is typed, replayable, or tied to the exact engine run that produced it.

### §9 and §10: the most important tests are future promises

The cex validity oracle in §9 is exactly the right test. It does not exist yet. M0 has not happened, and the spec explicitly says no engine-facing work should proceed before ADR-0003 (§10, M0; D13).

That makes the current project sequence backwards: substantial renderer and kernel work exists, while the central feasibility experiment remains undone.

## 5. Highest-value next move

Run M0 exactly as specified and write ADR-0003.

The spike should prove one real path:

1. private contracted function;
2. private method;
3. user-defined input;
4. same-crate `stub_verified`;
5. cross-crate failure;
6. generated in-crate harness;
7. real violation;
8. Kani playback;
9. playback rendered as a plain Rust test;
10. actual `ply-attrs` emission.

Do not add more renderer polish until this works.

Defer or cut:

- `synth` from the initial product;
- Verus translation;
- palette configuration;
- legend support;
- further collapse/routing polish;
- any feature whose only evidence is a diagram.

If M0 fails, amend the product around the actual supported Kani subset instead of building the rest of the architecture around paper semantics.

## 6. Dangerous or dishonest evidence modelling

Yes. The main dangers are these:

- `Evidence` can represent `Proved` without provenance. The kernel accepts a value; nothing forces the integration layer to prove where it came from.
- `conditional` assumptions are free-form strings. They can be missing, empty, duplicated, stale, or unrelated to the actual call graph.
- `open_items` excludes unresolved markers in the kernel, so the actual root count is split across two aggregation systems.
- A node can carry `Proved` plus `stale`, `unsupported`, `timeout`, or `conditional`. D6 says statuses are separate, which is defensible, but any consumer that reads only `evidence` will see a false green.
- `trusted` claims are unrestricted prose with no identity, timestamp, commit, scope, expiry, or machine-verifiable evidence reference. The shield is visually distinct, but it can still look like approval.
- Declared ceilings and earned verdicts share the same `Evidence` enum. The renderer carefully keeps them separate, but the type system does not. `component_verdict_node` is actually computing a ceiling, not a verdict (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:451-479`).
- The current ceiling calculation ignores inherited component checks, so even the “promise” visualization can be wrong.
- D7’s playback requirement is excellent, but until the cex oracle exists, a buggy adapter could emit a plausible-looking witness that does not reproduce.

## Prioritized top five changes

1. Execute M0 and make ADR-0003 authoritative.
2. Build one vertical slice: one inline contract → one Kani run → one parsed cex → one replayable test → one JSON diagnostic.
3. Separate declared ceilings, earned verdicts, statuses, assumptions, and provenance into distinct result types.
4. Implement the normative schema, merge rules, defaults, anchors, and fingerprints—or cut those claims from v1.
5. Freeze the renderer and defer synth, Verus, legends, palette work, and further visual polish until real verification evidence reaches the tree.

## Bottom line

Ply has a sound product boundary, but the project has validated the least risky 20% of it. Today it is a strong specification prototype, a useful static renderer, and a carefully tested aggregation kernel—not yet evidence that the proposed verification layer will work.

The core danger is semantic impedance at the engine boundary. “Glue” becomes a compiler: attributes, contracts, harnesses, generators, stubs, replay artifacts, engine parsers, fingerprints, and provenance all have to preserve meaning. That is much harder than subprocess orchestration.

## 1. Is “route to existing engines” sound?

Yes as a constraint. Building an SMT solver, model checker, or proof engine would destroy the project’s scope. The spec is right in §1: “We build glue and UX, never solvers.”

Where it breaks:

- Harness construction is the real product. Vetting 001 shows that `Spsc::try_push` and `try_pop` are not meaningfully fuzzable because the receiver is private, invariant-heavy state. A constructor hook only creates empty queues; it cannot generate reachable full states or call sequences. This is a fundamental gap, not a missing adapter.
- Contract semantics differ sharply between Kani, proptest, cargo-mutants, and Verus. `requires` as Kani assumptions, proptest rejection filters, and test preconditions are not interchangeable.
- Rust syntax and behavior exceed the contract subset quickly. Vetting 002 already found missing arrays and `matches!` bindings.
- `syn` extraction cannot make architecture enforcement sound. D4 admits this, but `strict: true` does not fix false negatives from macros, trait dispatch, aliases, generated code, or unresolved calls.
- D5’s modular composition becomes conditional for common cases: cross-crate calls, fuzzed callees, cycles, and generic instantiation mismatches. That may make the result technically honest but practically unattractive.
- Counterexample normalization is high-risk. D7 requires replayable Kani data plus stable Rust tests. The adapter must prove that the witness actually reproduces; currently no such pipeline exists.

The bet is viable only if M0 proves one narrow vertical slice with real Kani behavior. Until then, it is a strategy, not an established fact.

## 2. Was the verdict kernel the right first thing?

It was the right first pure artifact, but the wrong first product-risk test.

The kernel has genuinely good properties:

- `Evidence` makes the ordering explicit: `Violation < Unclaimed < Tested < Fuzzed < Bounded < Proved`.
- `Option<Evidence>` correctly distinguishes “no claimable descendants” from a real `Unclaimed` claim (`tools/kernel/src/lib.rs:155-170`).
- The exhaustive test checks 991,389 finite trees against an independent oracle. The fault-injection demo shows it catches both best-of aggregation and dropped assumptions.
- The implementation is small, pure, dependency-free, and easy to reason about.

But its scope is narrower than “Ply’s verdict semantics”:

- The enumeration covers trees of at most four nodes, 21 reduced node configurations, one representative status, and one fixed conditional assumption string (`tools/kernel/tests/enumeration.rs`).
- Kani harnesses are present but have not been run; `TODO.md:27-28` explicitly says to run them once Kani is installed.
- `unresolved` items are not represented in the kernel at all. The kernel comment says the model layer must add them later (`tools/kernel/src/lib.rs:131-139`), so the actual `open_items` contract remains unproved.
- Conditional assumptions are opaque strings, not linked to node IDs, callee hashes, verdict provenance, or actual call edges.
- `Some(vec![])` is representable, despite the comment claiming a conditional without assumptions is unrepresentable. The Kani harness never explores an empty assumption list because its symbolic shim always inserts one string.
- The “no rule sequence assigns one node two verdicts” obligation from `CLAUDE.md` is not modeled. The kernel returns one value by type construction; it does not test competing pipeline transitions, cached results, or merge behavior.
- The temporary `tmp_*` Kani harnesses are still in the production kernel (`tools/kernel/src/lib.rs:482+`).

So: good kernel, but it proves aggregation mechanics—not evidence authenticity, verdict lifecycle, or engine composition.

## 3. Is the visual grammar a good constraint?

Yes, mostly. It is not merely a gimmick.

It produced real design pressure:

- The black SVG failure exposed that structural tests were insufficient.
- `owns` was parsed but not rendered, violating the stated bijection.
- Vetting found unreadable glyphs, clipped deny rules, overlapping edges, and collisions inside nested boxes.
- The renderer now has useful invariants for style resolution, tooltips, canvas bounds, edge visibility, and overlap avoidance.

That justifies the constraint as a design gate for declarative architecture claims.

However, the current output does not justify the full product claim:

- It renders declared ceilings, not earned verdicts. The renderer explicitly says “none of it has been run yet” (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:749-764`).
- `strict` has no visual notch; it is only explained in a tooltip.
- `mode: synth` is parsed but has no visual treatment.
- `examples` are only mentioned in tooltips, not rendered as `e×N`.
- Statuses and actual verdicts are absent.
- Inline attributes are invisible because the renderer consumes YAML directly.
- `~>` flows are drawn while explicitly unverified in v1.

This directly contradicts the spec’s “Gate debt: none” claim. `TODO.md:25-26` still lists visual debt for `strict`, `mode: synth`, and `examples`.

The visual grammar is valuable for the architecture view. It should not become the project’s main development surface before an actual engine-produced counterexample exists.

## 4. Where is the spec self-deceiving or vague?

### §5 and D3: the “normative schema” does not exist

The spec says `schema/ply.schema.json` is normative, embedded, versioned, and golden-tested (§5, D3). The repository has no schema and no schema validator. `ply-model` only uses serde structs (`tools/model/src/lib.rs:12-110`).

Current gaps include:

- no `ply: 1` version enforcement;
- no identifier validation for component/profile names;
- no line/column mapping;
- no multi-file merge;
- no duplicate component detection across files;
- no nearest-key suggestions;
- no profile-reference validation;
- no robust deny/edge lexical validation.

The current `parse_deny` accepts surprisingly loose input (`tools/model/src/lib.rs:235-258`). That is not yet a normative grammar.

### §5.1: component default checks are underspecified and currently ignored

The spec says component-level `checks` are “default checks for all fns in scope” (§5.1, line 225). But `tools/check/src/lib.rs:95-99` explicitly does not merge inherited and function checks, and the renderer’s ceiling computation only reads `fc.checks` (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:434-448`).

This means declared ceilings and eventual verification behavior can disagree before any engine is involved.

### §5.2 and D14: fingerprints are underspecified

“Token-stream hash, merged contract text, callee contract hashes, engine version, flags, features, target” is not enough to define reproducibility.

Unanswered questions:

- Which rustc version?
- Which dependency lockfile?
- Which macro expansion?
- Which environment variables?
- Which generated harness?
- Which `trusted` evidence?
- Which generator implementation?
- How are equivalent contract expressions normalized?
- What exactly does `accept` attest to?

Without a precise lock format and provenance model, stale detection becomes ceremonial.

### §5.3: advisory extraction is honest, but not architecture enforcement

D4 correctly admits that `syn` is approximate. But a `strict` switch cannot turn incomplete extraction into a sound security boundary. The spec should say plainly:

> strict means “fail on detected violations,” not “prove no violation exists.”

The current tools have none of the extractor, coverage metric, anchor resolution, or architecture engine promised by §5.3 and D11.

### §5.4a–§5.4c: the contract system is still a collection of assumptions

Vetting 001 and 002 already found:

- stateful receiver generation is missing;
- arrays are omitted from §5.4b;
- `matches!` bindings are not defined;
- `old()` only covers a narrow two-state case;
- sequence properties and FIFO ordering remain inexpressible;
- test generation from contracts is not specified enough to know what inputs it can produce;
- high-rejection behavior for `requires` has no defined threshold or verdict effect.

The subset can be made useful, but it must be presented as a deliberately small fragment, not as a general contract language.

### §5.5 / D5: conditional evidence is structurally visible but semantically weak

The spec says the caller is conditional when it assumes a callee contract. It does not define:

- how the assumption is tied to a concrete callee claim;
- how generic instantiations are matched;
- how the caller’s precondition proves the callee’s `requires`;
- how stale or weak callee contracts affect the caller;
- how an assumption is discharged later.

Vetting 002 explicitly leaves generic reuse and instantiation mismatch unresolved.

### §7.1 and §8: the renderer contract is internally inconsistent

§7.1 says the renderer’s only input is the §8 envelope. The actual renderer takes `ply_model::Document`, runs `ply-check` itself, and computes declared ceilings directly (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:1666+`).

That is acceptable for a prototype renderer, but it does not validate the future envelope contract.

§8 itself is only an example JSON object, not a schema. The counterexample fields are mostly strings, and there is no formal guarantee that a reported witness is typed, replayable, or tied to the exact engine run that produced it.

### §9 and §10: the most important tests are future promises

The cex validity oracle in §9 is exactly the right test. It does not exist yet. M0 has not happened, and the spec explicitly says no engine-facing work should proceed before ADR-0003 (§10, M0; D13).

That makes the current project sequence backwards: substantial renderer and kernel work exists, while the central feasibility experiment remains undone.

## 5. Highest-value next move

Run M0 exactly as specified and write ADR-0003.

The spike should prove one real path:

1. private contracted function;
2. private method;
3. user-defined input;
4. same-crate `stub_verified`;
5. cross-crate failure;
6. generated in-crate harness;
7. real violation;
8. Kani playback;
9. playback rendered as a plain Rust test;
10. actual `ply-attrs` emission.

Do not add more renderer polish until this works.

Defer or cut:

- `synth` from the initial product;
- Verus translation;
- palette configuration;
- legend support;
- further collapse/routing polish;
- any feature whose only evidence is a diagram.

If M0 fails, amend the product around the actual supported Kani subset instead of building the rest of the architecture around paper semantics.

## 6. Dangerous or dishonest evidence modelling

Yes. The main dangers are these:

- `Evidence` can represent `Proved` without provenance. The kernel accepts a value; nothing forces the integration layer to prove where it came from.
- `conditional` assumptions are free-form strings. They can be missing, empty, duplicated, stale, or unrelated to the actual call graph.
- `open_items` excludes unresolved markers in the kernel, so the actual root count is split across two aggregation systems.
- A node can carry `Proved` plus `stale`, `unsupported`, `timeout`, or `conditional`. D6 says statuses are separate, which is defensible, but any consumer that reads only `evidence` will see a false green.
- `trusted` claims are unrestricted prose with no identity, timestamp, commit, scope, expiry, or machine-verifiable evidence reference. The shield is visually distinct, but it can still look like approval.
- Declared ceilings and earned verdicts share the same `Evidence` enum. The renderer carefully keeps them separate, but the type system does not. `component_verdict_node` is actually computing a ceiling, not a verdict (`tools/render/src/svg.rs:451-479`).
- The current ceiling calculation ignores inherited component checks, so even the “promise” visualization can be wrong.
- D7’s playback requirement is excellent, but until the cex oracle exists, a buggy adapter could emit a plausible-looking witness that does not reproduce.

## Prioritized top five changes

1. Execute M0 and make ADR-0003 authoritative.
2. Build one vertical slice: one inline contract → one Kani run → one parsed cex → one replayable test → one JSON diagnostic.
3. Separate declared ceilings, earned verdicts, statuses, assumptions, and provenance into distinct result types.
4. Implement the normative schema, merge rules, defaults, anchors, and fingerprints—or cut those claims from v1.
5. Freeze the renderer and defer synth, Verus, legends, palette work, and further visual polish until real verification evidence reaches the tree.
